between np-games
G
and
H
is the t-skeleton

\uplus\phi\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\begin{cases}\top\,(=\{\bm{\epsilon}\})&\text{if $\phi=\emptyset$;}\\
\bigcup_{(\gamma,e)\in\mathscr{R}^{\mathrm{cp}}_{\mathrm{wr}}(G)}\phi_{(\gamma,e)}^{[e]}&\text{otherwise,}\end{cases}

for an FoWRWLI
\phi
between
G
and
H
, where

\phi_{(\gamma,e)}^{[e]}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\{\,(\bm{s}(1),e).(\bm{s}(2),e)\dots(\bm{s}(|\bm{s}|),e)\mid\bm{s}\in\phi_{(\gamma,e)}\,\},

and the ‘tags’
(\_,e)
are implemented within the formalization of standard moves.

Given an np-game
K
and a DoWRWLI
\uplus\psi
between
H
and
K
, the
composition
of
\uplus\phi
and
\uplus\psi
is the DoWRWLI
\uplus\psi\circ\uplus\phi
between
G
and
K
defined by

\uplus\psi\circ\uplus\phi\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\uplus(\psi\circ\phi).

Remark
.

Alternatively, a DoWRWLI
\uplus\phi
between np-games
G
and
H
can be defined by
\uplus\phi\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\top
if
\phi=\emptyset
, and
\uplus\phi\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\bigcup_{e\in\pi_{2}(\mathscr{R}^{\mathrm{cp}}_{\mathrm{wr}}(G))}\phi_{e}^{[e]}
, where
\phi_{e}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\bigcup_{(\gamma,e)\in\mathscr{R}^{\mathrm{cp}}_{\mathrm{wr}}(G)}\phi_{(\gamma,e)}
, otherwise.
This point on DoWRWLIs, which are morphisms in our category, justifies the simplification of the semantics of pi-types made in Section
1.10
, as we shall see.

Convention
.

Henceforth, we shall frequently reason about DoWRWLIs, for which we need case analyses accordingly to how DoWRWLIs are defined.
It is, however, mostly rather trivial to handle the first case (i.e., when the underlying FoWRWLI
\phi
of a given DoWRWLI
\uplus\phi
is empty), and so we shall often skip it.

Lemma 3.12

(Well-defined DoWRWLIs)
.

Let
G
,
H
and
K
be np-games.

1.

Any DoWRWLI between
G
and
H
is a t-skeleton contained in
\mathscr{J}(\mathscr{M})
;

2.

DoWRWLIs (resp. winning, recursive ones) are closed under composition.

Proof.

For the first clause, it suffices to remark that each DoWRWLI is deterministic thanks to the second axiom on FoWRWLIs.

For the second clause, note that each DoWRWLI uniquely determines the underlying FoWRWLI so that the composition is well-defined on DoWRWLIs (resp. winning ones).
Finally, the third axiom on FoWRWLIs or ‘effectivity’ of realizer-maps enables us to ‘effectively’ obtain the ‘intermediate’ realizer
\pi_{\phi}(e)
on the component
(\psi\circ\phi)_{e}
of the composition
\uplus\psi\circ\uplus\phi
of given composable DoWRWLIs
\uplus\phi
and
\uplus\psi
so that the composition evidently preserves recursiveness of DoWRWLIs (by the same argument for closure of recursive strategies under composition in
[
47
, p. 355]
).
∎

For comparison with DoWRWLIs, let us recall
UoPLIs

[
35
]
between p-games:

Definition 3.13

(UoPLIs
[
35
]
)
.

A
family of pointwise linear implications (FoPLI)
between p-games
G
and
H
is a family
\varphi=(\varphi_{\sigma})_{\sigma::G}
of standard t-skeletons
\varphi_{\sigma}
that satisfies

1.

\forall\sigma::G.\,\exists\tau::H.\,\varphi_{\sigma}::\sigma\multimap\tau
;

2.

\forall\sigma,\tilde{\sigma}::G,\bm{s}mn\in\varphi_{\sigma}^{\mathrm{Even}},\bm{s}m\tilde{n}\in\varphi_{\tilde{\sigma}}^{\mathrm{Even}}.\,\bm{s}mn=\bm{s}m\tilde{n}
.

We write
\mathscr{F}(G,H)
for the set of all FoPLIs between
G
and
H
, and call the union
\bigcup\varphi\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\bigcup_{\sigma::G}\varphi_{\sigma}
the
union of PLIs (UoPLI)
between
G
and
H
(on
\varphi
).

The first axiom on an FoPLI specifies the induced UoPLI in the
pointwise
fashion.
On the other hand, the second axiom brings UoPLIs
determinacy
so that they are well-defined t-skeletons.
Moreover, the second axiom gives rise to the
computational nature
or
domain-theoretic structures
of game semantics, e.g., thanks to the axiom, the input-output pairs (or extension) of a UoPLI induce
continuous
functions
[
35
]
.

Crucially, DoWRWLIs differ from UoPLIs in the following two points:

1.

O in each DoWRWLI must play on the domain by a
winning, recursive
t-skeleton and
exhibit the canonical realizer
for it by the ‘tag’ at his first move, while in each UoPLI O may play by any t-skeleton on the domain and does not have to exhibit anything other than ordinary O-moves during a play;

2.

A play by a DoWRWLI may
depend on the realizer
that O supplies, while it is not the case for a play by a UoPLI due to its second axiom.

Consequently, DoWRWLIs implement our idea on how to validate CT, which is described in Section
1.10
, while UoPLIs cannot.

Next, recall that the previous work
[
35
]
defines the
linear implication

G\multimap H
between p-games
G
and
H
by
G\multimap H\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\{\,\bigcup\Phi\mid\Phi\in\mathscr{F}(G,H)\,\}
.
Similarly, we define our modified linear implication
\rightarrowtriangle
between np-games by

Definition 3.14

(Winning-realizer-wise linear implication)
.

The
winning-realizer-wise (w.r.w.) linear implication
between np-games
G
and
H
is the np-game

G\rightarrowtriangle H\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\{\,\uplus\phi\mid\phi\in\mathscr{F}_{\mathrm{wrw}}(G,H)\,\}.

Note that
G\rightarrowtriangle H=\emptyset
if
\mathscr{R}^{\mathrm{cp}}_{\mathrm{wr}}(G)\neq\emptyset
and
\mathscr{R}^{\mathrm{cp}}_{\mathrm{wr}}(H)=\emptyset
.
It is why we allow an np-game to be the empty set (Definition
3.8
).

Lemma 3.15

(Well-defined winning-realizer-wise linear implication)
.

Np-games are closed under w.r.w. linear implication
\rightarrowtriangle
.

Proof.

Immediate from the first clause of Lemma
3.12
.
∎

On the other hand, p-games are not closed under w.r.w. linear implication
\rightarrowtriangle
.
To see this point, note that the ‘tags’ for DoWRWLIs are embedded into standard moves, and thus
certain forms of standard moves determine O’s play on the domain
of a DoWRWLI.
Consequently, p-games are not closed under w.r.w. linear implication since it does not preserve
saturation
of positions.
For instance, consider the w.r.w. linear implication
\oc(\oc N\rightarrowtriangle N)\rightarrowtriangle N
, where
N
and
\oc N
are the natural number game and its exponential (Section
1.7
) regarded as p-games.
A play in it looks like

\oc(\oc N

\rightarrowtriangle

N)

\rightarrowtriangle

N

q_{\oc(\oc N\rightarrowtriangle N)\rightarrowtriangle N}

\uplus\phi

q^{[e]}

(q,j)^{[e^{\prime}],[e]}

((q,i),j)^{[e^{\prime}],[e]}

((n,i),j)^{[e^{\prime}],[e]}

(m,j)^{[e^{\prime}],[e]}

(q,j^{\prime})^{[e^{\prime}],[e]}

((q,i^{\prime}),j^{\prime})^{[e^{\prime}],[e]}

((n^{\prime},i^{\prime}),j^{\prime})^{[e^{\prime}],[e]}

(m^{\prime},j^{\prime})^{[e^{\prime}],[e]}

l^{[e]}

Because
e
realizes O’s play on the domain
\oc N\rightarrowtriangle N
, his choice
i\in\mathbb{N}
is determined by
e
, where the ‘tag’
(\_)^{[e]}
constitutes the moves in the diagram.
It then follows that the set
P_{\oc(\oc N\rightarrowtriangle N)\rightarrowtriangle N}
is not saturated (as
i
does not range over all natural numbers unless we change
e
), showing that
\oc(\oc N\rightarrowtriangle N)\rightarrowtriangle N
is not a p-game.

We circumvent this problem by discarding identifications and the saturation axiom on p-games, and then adopting np-games and
simplified exponential

\hat{\oc}
on them:

Definition 3.16

(Simplified exponential on nonstandard predicative games)
.

The
simplified exponential
of an np-game
G
is the np-game
\hat{\oc}G\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\{\,\hat{\oc}\sigma\mid\sigma::G\,\}
.

Notation
.

We adopt the notations for exponential
\oc
on games given at the end of Section
2.1
also for simplified exponential
\hat{\oc}
on np-games in the evident way.
It in particular implies that
\hat{\oc}\sigma=\sigma^{\dagger}::\hat{\oc}G
holds for any np-game
G
and t-skeleton
\sigma::G
.

Remark
.

The previous work
[
35
]
necessitates the
countably infinite tensor
on t-skeletons
(\sigma_{i})_{i\in\mathbb{N}}
for exponential
\oc
on p-games in order to systematically accommodate
non-innocent
t-skeletons on exponentials.
In contrast, we shall use simplified exponential
\hat{\oc}
on np-games exclusively on the domains of w.r.w. linear implications, where O must play by
innocent
t-skeletons which are all
promotions
.
Hence, it suffices to define simplified exponential on np-games as above.

Let us proceed to define other constructions on np-games:

Definition 3.17

(Winning-realizer-wise implication)
.

The
winning-realizer-wise (w.r.w.) implication
between np-games
G
and
H
is the np-game
\hat{\oc}G\rightarrowtriangle H
.

Definition 3.18

(Product on nonstandard predicative games)
.

The
product
of np-games
G
and
H
is the np-game
G\mathbin{\&}H\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\{\langle\sigma,\tau\rangle\mid\sigma::,\tau::H\,\}
.

Recall once again that McCusker
[
33
,
36
]
has to focus on well-opened games for the use of simplified exponential
\hat{\oc}
on games.
For the same reason, we shall henceforth focus on well-opened np-games, which the following theorem supports:

Theorem 3.19

(Well-defined constructions on nonstandard predicative games)
.

Np-games (resp. well-opened ones) are closed under product and w.r.w. implication.

Proof.

Closure of np-games (resp. well-opened ones) under product is obvious, and that under implication follows immediately from Lemma
3.15
.
∎

At this point, we can sketch how we shall validate CT as follows.
Consider the np-game
\hat{\oc}(\hat{\oc}N\rightarrowtriangle N)\rightarrowtriangle N
, which interprets the type
\mathsf{(N\Rightarrow N)\Rightarrow N}
.
We then define a t-skeleton
\uplus\mathrm{ctl}:\hat{\oc}(\hat{\oc}N\rightarrowtriangle N)\rightarrowtriangle N
that plays as in the following diagram:

\hat{\oc}(\hat{\oc}N

\rightarrowtriangle

N)

\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\uplus\mathrm{ctl}}}{{\rightarrowtriangle}}

N

q_{\hat{\oc}(\hat{\oc}N\rightarrowtriangle N)\rightarrowtriangle N}

\uplus\mathrm{ctl}

q^{[e]}

(e^{\ddagger})^{[e]}

Note that O must perform the first move
q^{[e]}
in
\hat{\oc}(\hat{\oc}N\rightarrowtriangle N)\rightarrowtriangle N
, which specifies the canonical realizer
e\in\pi_{2}(\mathscr{R}^{\mathrm{cp}}_{\mathrm{wr}}(\hat{\oc}(\hat{\oc}N\rightarrowtriangle N)))
for his intended computation on the domain
\hat{\oc}(\hat{\oc}N\rightarrowtriangle N)
.
The computation must be by a winning, recursive t-skeleton on
\hat{\oc}(\hat{\oc}N\rightarrowtriangle N)
.
Then, the t-skeleton
\uplus\mathrm{ctl}
directs P to ‘copy-cat’ the given realizer
e
as the P-move
(e^{\ddagger})^{[e]}
, where
e^{\ddagger}
realizes the winning, recursive t-skeleton on
\hat{\oc}N\rightarrowtriangle N
that essentially coincides with the one realized by
e
.
In this way, the t-skeleton
\uplus\mathrm{ctl}
models the nontrivial part of CT (
2
), which is the main idea of the present work.

Moreover, it is straightforward to see that the t-skeleton
\uplus\mathrm{ctl}
is
recursive
so that we may
constructively
model MLTT plus CT.
Note that the position
q^{[e]}\in\uplus\mathrm{ctl}
is not even partially decidable since otherwise it would contradict the well-known fact that the set of all total recursive functions is not recursively enumerable; for this point, we have carefully ‘compromised’ our notions of (the ‘effectivity’ of) recursive t-skeletons and realizer-maps, as already remarked, so that
\uplus\mathrm{ctl}
is recursive.

Also, let us emphasize the point that the t-skeleton
\uplus\mathrm{ctl}
resolves the
dilemma between intensionality and extensionality
explained in Section
1.6
.
In fact, O must exhibit the canonical realizer for his winning, recursive t-skeleton on the domain
on the nose
at his first move in a play in
\uplus\mathrm{ctl}
, which is very intensional like realizability à la Kleene, while
\uplus\mathrm{ctl}
itself is just as abstract as morphisms in the game semantics
[
35
]
or realizability à la assemblies, i.e., extensional enough to model MLTT.

The rest of this article is dedicated to verifying this solution in detail.
Towards this end, we first modify constructions on t-skeletons on linear implication
\multimap

[
35
]
into appropriate ones on w.r.w. linear implication
\rightarrowtriangle
:

Definition 3.20

(Winning-realizer-wise copy-cats)
.

The
winning-realizer-wise (w.r.w.) copy-cat
on an np-game
G
is the DoWRWLI
\uplus\mathrm{cp}_{G}::G\rightarrowtriangle G
whose component
(\mathrm{cp}_{G})_{(\gamma,e)}
for each
(\gamma,e)\in\mathscr{R}^{\mathrm{cp}}_{\mathrm{wr}}(G)
is given by

(\mathrm{cp}_{G})_{(\gamma,e)}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\{\,\bm{s}\in\gamma^{[0]}\multimap\gamma^{[1]}\mid\forall\bm{t}\preceq{\bm{s}}.\ \mathrm{Even}(\bm{t})\Rightarrow\bm{t}\upharpoonright\gamma^{[0]}=\bm{t}\upharpoonright\gamma^{[1]}\,\},

where the superscripts
(\_)^{[i]}
(
i=0,1
) are to distinguish the two copies of
\gamma
.

Definition 3.21

(Winning-realizer-wise derelictions)
.

The
winning-realizer-wise (w.r.w.) dereliction
on an np-game
G
is the DoWRWLI
\uplus\mathrm{der}_{G}::\hat{\oc}G\rightarrowtriangle G
whose component
(\mathrm{der}_{G})_{(\gamma^{\dagger},e^{\prime})}
for each
(\gamma^{\dagger},e^{\prime})\in\mathscr{R}^{\mathrm{cp}}_{\mathrm{wr}}(\hat{\oc}G)
is given by

(\mathrm{der}_{G})_{(\gamma^{\dagger},e^{\prime})}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\{\,\bm{s}\in\gamma^{\dagger}\multimap\gamma\mid\forall\bm{t}\preceq{\bm{s}}.\ \mathrm{Even}(\bm{t})\Rightarrow\bm{t}\upharpoonright\gamma^{\dagger}=\bm{t}\upharpoonright\gamma\,\}.

Lemma 3.22

(Well-defined winning-realizer-wise copy-cats and derelictions)
.

Let
G
be an np-game.

1.

The w.r.w. copy-cat
\uplus\mathrm{cp}_{G}
is a winning, recursive t-skeleton on the np-game
G\rightarrowtriangle G
, and it is the unit for composition on DoWRWLIs;

2.

The w.r.w. dereliction
\uplus\mathrm{der}_{G}
is a winning, recursive t-skeleton on the np-game
\hat{\oc}G\rightarrowtriangle G
if
G
is well-opened.

Proof.

Similar to the case of copy-cats and derelictions given in
[
35
]
, where
\uplus\mathrm{cp}_{G}
and
\uplus\mathrm{der}_{G}
are clearly recursive, and they are the trivial t-skeleton
\top
if
\mathscr{R}_{\mathrm{wr}}^{\mathrm{cp}}(G)=\emptyset
.
∎

Definition 3.23

(Constructions on DoWRWLIs)
.

Given np-games
A
,
B
,
C
and
D
, and DoWRWLIs
\uplus\phi::A\rightarrowtriangle B
,
\uplus\rho::A\rightarrowtriangle C
and
\uplus\varphi::\hat{\oc}A\rightarrowtriangle B
, we define

•

The
pairing

\langle\uplus\phi,\uplus\rho\rangle\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\uplus\langle\phi,\rho\rangle::A\rightarrowtriangle B\mathbin{\&}C
of
\uplus\phi
and
\uplus\rho
by

\langle\phi,\rho\rangle_{(\gamma,e)}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\langle\phi_{(\gamma,e)},\rho_{(\gamma,e)}\rangle

for each
(\gamma,e)\in\mathscr{R}^{\mathrm{cp}}_{\mathrm{wr}}(A)
;

•

The
promotion

(\uplus\varphi)^{\dagger}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\uplus(\varphi^{\dagger})::\hat{\oc}A\rightarrowtriangle\hat{\oc}B
of
\uplus\varphi
by

(\varphi^{\dagger})_{(\gamma^{\dagger},e^{\prime})}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}(\varphi_{(\gamma^{\dagger},e^{\prime})})^{\dagger}

for each
(\gamma^{\dagger},e^{\prime})\in\mathscr{R}^{\mathrm{cp}}_{\mathrm{wr}}(\hat{\oc}A)
(which allows us to write
\uplus\varphi^{\dagger}
for
(\uplus\varphi)^{\dagger}
),

where we (can and do) assume that the corresponding constructions on realizer-maps are ‘effective’ by an appropriate choice of canonical realizers (Definition
3.4
).

Remark
.

We fix
canonical
realizers since they enable us to concisely define these constructions on DoWRWLIs in such a way that Lemma
3.25
below holds.

Lemma 3.24

(Well-defined constructions on DoWRWLIs)
.

DoWRWLIs (resp. winning, recursive ones) are closed under pairing and promotion.

Proof.

Similar to the corresponding constructions on UoPLIs
[
35
]
, where recursive DoWRWLIs are clearly closed under pairing and promotion.
∎

Lemma 3.25

(Winning-realizer-wise promotion lemma)
.

Let
A
,
B
,
C
and
D
be np-games, and
\uplus\phi::\hat{\oc}A\rightarrowtriangle B
,
\uplus\psi::\hat{\oc}B\rightarrowtriangle C
and
\uplus\varphi::\hat{\oc}C\rightarrowtriangle D
DoWRWLIs.

1.

\uplus\mathrm{der}_{A}^{\dagger}=\uplus\mathrm{cp}_{\hat{\oc}A}::\hat{\oc}A\rightarrowtriangle\hat{\oc}A
and
\uplus\mathrm{der}_{B}\circ\uplus\phi^{\dagger}=\uplus\phi::\hat{\oc}A\rightarrowtriangle B
;

2.

\uplus\varphi\circ(\uplus\psi\circ\uplus\phi^{\dagger})^{\dagger}=(\uplus\varphi\circ\uplus\psi^{\dagger})\circ\uplus\phi^{\dagger}::\hat{\oc}A\rightarrowtriangle D
.

Proof.

Similar to the case of UoPLIs
[
35
]
, where it is crucial for the first and the second clauses to fix a choice of canonical realizers (Definition
3.4
).
∎

We are now ready to define:

Definition 3.26

(Category of realizability à la game semantics)
.

The category
\mathbb{NPG}_{\mathrm{wrw}}^{\mathrm{wo}}
consists of

•

Well-opened np-games as objects;

•

Winning, recursive t-skeletons on
\hat{\oc}A\rightarrowtriangle B
as morphisms
A\rightarrow B
;

•

The composition
\uplus\psi\bullet\uplus\phi:A\rightarrow C
of morphisms
\uplus\phi:A\rightarrow B
and
\uplus\psi:B\rightarrow C
given by
\uplus\psi\bullet\uplus\phi\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\uplus\psi\circ\uplus\phi^{\dagger}
;

•

W.r.w. derelictions
\uplus\mathrm{der}_{A}:\hat{\oc}A\rightarrowtriangle A
as identities
A\rightarrow A
.

The category
\mathbb{NPG}_{\mathrm{wrw}}^{\mathrm{wo}}
is well-defined by Lemmata
3.12
,
3.22
,
3.24
and
3.25
, and moreover
cartesian closed
(which is an immediate corollary of the theorem given later that
\mathbb{NPG}_{\mathrm{wrw}}^{\mathrm{wo}}
models MLTT equipped with unit-, pi- and sigma-types via a CwF and semantic type formers, and so the following proof is just a sketch):

Proposition 3.27

(Cartesian closure)
.

The category
\mathbb{NPG}_{\mathrm{wrw}}^{\mathrm{wo}}
is cartesian closed, where a terminal object, products and exponential objects are given by the unit np-game
\bm{1}
, product
\&
and w.r.w. implication
\hat{\oc}(\_)\rightarrowtriangle(\_)
, respectively.

Proof (sketch).

By Theorem
3.19
and Lemmata
3.24
and
3.25
, where note that t-skeletons in
\mathbb{NPG}_{\mathrm{wrw}}^{\mathrm{wo}}
are all winning and recursive so that
\mathbb{NPG}_{\mathrm{wrw}}^{\mathrm{wo}}
is closed.
∎

Notation
.

Given
G\in\mathbb{NPG}_{\mathrm{wrw}}^{\mathrm{wo}}
, we write
\mathbb{NPG}_{\mathrm{wrw}}^{\mathrm{wo}}(G)
for the hom-set
\mathbb{NPG}_{\mathrm{wrw}}^{\mathrm{wo}}(\bm{1},G)
.
Hence, each element of
\mathbb{NPG}_{\mathrm{wrw}}^{\mathrm{wo}}(G)
is of the form
\uplus\{\gamma_{0}^{\hat{\oc}\bm{1}}\}
for some
\gamma_{0}\in\mathcal{TS}_{\mathrm{wr}}(G)
(n.b.,
\gamma_{0}::\Gamma
implies
\gamma_{0}^{\hat{\oc}\bm{1}}::\top^{\dagger}\rightarrowtriangle\Gamma
); we usually write
\uplus\gamma
for it (i.e.,
\gamma=\{\gamma_{0}^{\hat{\oc}\bm{1}}\}
).

Just for convenience, we also write
\mathbb{NPG}_{\mathrm{wrw}}(H)
for the set of all winning, recursive t-skeletons on
\hat{\oc}\bm{1}\rightarrowtriangle H
for any (not necessarily well-opened) np-game
H
.

Similarly to the game semantics of simple type theories and the game semantics of MLTT
[
35
]
, the w.r.w. implications
\hat{\oc}(A\mathbin{\&}B)\rightarrowtriangle C
and
\hat{\oc}A\rightarrowtriangle(\hat{\oc}B\rightarrowtriangle C)
coincide up to ‘tags’ on moves for any np-games
A
,
B
and
C
(n.b., t-skeletons on the w.r.w. implications are only the trivial one
\top
if
\mathscr{R}_{\mathrm{wr}}^{\mathrm{cp}}(A)=\emptyset
or
\mathscr{R}_{\mathrm{wr}}^{\mathrm{cp}}(B)=\emptyset
), and therefore currying is modeled in the category
\mathbb{NPG}_{\mathrm{wrw}}^{\mathrm{wo}}
.
In particular, it explains essentially why our model of MLTT in
\mathbb{NPG}_{\mathrm{wrw}}^{\mathrm{wo}}
validates the
\xi
-rule, as we shall see shortly.

3.3
Realizability model of intensional Martin-Löf type theory equipped with formal Church’s thesis à la game semantics

In this section, we lift the CCC
\mathbb{NPG}_{\mathrm{wrw}}^{\mathrm{wo}}
to a
category with families (CwF)

[
20
,
50
]
, an abstract model of MLTT, equipped with
semantic type formers

[
20
]
for unit-, empty-, N-, pi-, sigma- and Id-types so that
\mathbb{NPG}_{\mathrm{wrw}}^{\mathrm{wo}}
is shown to model MLTT together with these types by
soundness
of the semantics of MLTT in CwFs
[
20
]
.

Towards this end, let us first introduce our interpretation of (dependent) types:

Definition 3.28

(Dependent nonstandard predicative games)
.

A
dependent nonstandard predicative (np-) game
on an np-game
\Gamma
is a family

A=(A(\uplus\gamma))_{\uplus\gamma\in\mathbb{NPG}_{\mathrm{wrw}}(\Gamma)}

of np-games
A(\uplus\gamma)
such that the union
\bigcup_{\uplus\gamma\in\mathbb{NPG}_{\mathrm{wrw}}(\Gamma)}\mathbin{\vdash_{A(\uplus\gamma)}}
is well-founded.

A dependent np-game is
well-opened
if so are all its components.
We write
\mathscr{D}\mathbb{NPG}_{\mathrm{wrw}}^{\mathrm{wo}}(\Gamma)
for the set of all well-opened dependent np-games on
\Gamma
.

Unlike the game semantics of MLTT
[
35
]
, the indexing t-skeletons of a dependent np-game
A
on an np-game
\Gamma
are on
\Gamma
, not
\hat{\oc}\Gamma
, but it does not lose any generality for our interpretation since the domain and the codomain t-skeletons of DoWRWLIs are all
innocent
so that we may assume that those on
\hat{\oc}\Gamma
are all
promotions
.

Let us then generalize w.r.w. implication (Definition
3.17
) in such a way that it captures type dependency of pi-types (Definition
3.31
):

Definition 3.29

(Integration on dependent nonstandard predicative games)
.

The
integration
of a dependent np-game
A
on an np-game
\Gamma
is the np-game

\int_{\Gamma}A\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\bigcup_{\uplus\gamma\in\mathbb{NPG}_{\mathrm{wrw}}(\Gamma)}A(\uplus\gamma)\quad\text{(n.b., we often abbreviate $\int_{\Gamma}$ as $\int$)}.

Remark
.

The integration on dependent np-games is simpler than the corresponding one on p-games in the previous work
[
35
]
as ours does not have to be det-j complete.

Definition 3.30

(FoDWRWLIs)
.

A
family of dependently winning-realizer-wise linear implications (FoDWRWLI)
from an np-game
\Gamma
to a dependent np-game
A
on
\Gamma
is an FoWRWLI
\phi
from
\Gamma
to
\int_{\Gamma}A
that satisfies

\forall(\gamma,e)\in\mathscr{R}^{\mathrm{cp}}_{\mathrm{wr}}(\Gamma).\,\mathrm{cod}_{\phi}(\gamma,e)::A(\uplus\{\gamma^{\hat{\oc}\bm{1}}\})\quad\text{(n.b., $\gamma::\Gamma$ implies $\gamma^{\hat{\oc}\bm{1}}::\top^{\dagger}\rightarrowtriangle\Gamma$)}.

We write
\mathscr{F_{\mathrm{wrw}}}(\Gamma,A)
for the set of all FoDWRWLIs from
\Gamma
to
A
.

Definition 3.31

(Winning-realizer-wise linear-pi and pi)
.

The
winning-realizer-wise (w.r.w.) linear-pi
from an np-game
\Gamma
to a dependent np-game
A
on
\Gamma
is the np-game

\ell\Pi_{\mathrm{wrw}}(\Gamma,A)\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\{\,\uplus\phi\mid\phi\in\mathscr{F}_{\mathrm{wrw}}(\Gamma,A)\,\},

and the
winning-realizer-wise (w.r.w.) pi
from
\Gamma
to
A
is the np-game

\Pi_{\mathrm{wrw}}(\Gamma,A)\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\ell\Pi_{\mathrm{wrw}}(\hat{\oc}\Gamma,A^{\ddagger}),

where
A^{\ddagger}
is the dependent np-game on
\hat{\oc}\Gamma
defined by
A^{\ddagger}(\uplus\gamma^{\dagger})\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}A(\uplus\gamma)
for each
\uplus\gamma^{\dagger}\in\mathbb{NPG}_{\mathrm{wrw}}(\hat{\oc}\Gamma)
.

Lemma 3.32

(Well-defined winning-realizer-wise linear-pi and pi)
.

The w.r.w. linear-pi
\ell\Pi_{\mathrm{wrw}}(\Gamma,A)
and the w.r.w. pi
\Pi_{\mathrm{wrw}}(\Gamma,A)
are (well-opened) np-games for any np-game
\Gamma
and (well-opened) dependent np-game
A
on
\Gamma
.

Proof.

Immediate from Lemma
3.15
and Theorem
3.19
.
∎

Let us similarly define the w.r.w. variant of sigma:

Definition 3.33

(Winning-realizer-wise sigma)
.

The
winning-realizer-wise (w.r.w.) sigma
of an np-game
\Gamma
and a dependent np-game
A
on
\Gamma
is the np-game

\Sigma_{\mathrm{wrw}}(\Gamma,A)\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\{\langle\gamma,\alpha\rangle::\Gamma\mathbin{\&}\int_{\Gamma}A\mid\gamma\in\mathcal{TS}_{\mathrm{wr}}(\Gamma)\Rightarrow\alpha::A(\uplus\{\gamma^{\hat{\oc}\bm{1}}\})\,\}.

Lemma 3.34

(Well-defined winning-realizer-wise sigma)
.

The w.r.w. sigma
\Sigma_{\mathrm{wrw}}(\Gamma,A)
is a (well-opened) np-game for any (well-opened) np-game
\Gamma
and (well-opened) dependent np-game
A
on
\Gamma
.

Proof.

Immediate from Theorem
3.19
.
∎

Finally, let us introduce our interpretation of Id-types:

Definition 3.35

(Identity nonstandard predicative games)
.

Given an np-game
\Gamma
and t-skeletons
\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}::\Gamma
, the
identity (Id)
between
\gamma
and
\gamma^{\prime}
is the np-game

\mathrm{Id}_{\Gamma}(\gamma,\gamma^{\prime})\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\begin{cases}\bm{1}&\text{if $\gamma=\gamma^{\prime}$;}\\
\bm{0}&\text{otherwise.}\end{cases}

Lemma 3.36

(Well-defined Id on nonstandard predicative games)
.

Given an np-game
\Gamma
, the Id
\mathrm{Id}_{\Gamma}(\gamma,\gamma^{\prime})
between any
\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}::\Gamma
is a well-opened np-game.

Proof.

Obvious.
∎

We are now ready to lift the CCC
\mathbb{NPG}_{\mathrm{wrw}}^{\mathrm{wo}}
to a CwF.
Let us first recall the general definition of CwFs
[
50
]
, where our presentation is based on
[
20
]
:

Definition 3.37

(Categories with families
[
20
,
50
]
)
.

A
category with families (CwF)
is a tuple
\mathcal{C}=(\mathcal{C},\mathrm{Ty},\mathrm{Tm},\_\{\_\},T,\_.\_,\mathrm{p},\mathrm{v},\langle\_,\_\rangle_{\_})
,
where

•

\mathcal{C}
is a category with a terminal object
T\in\mathcal{C}
;

•

\mathrm{Ty}
assigns, to each object
\Gamma\in\mathcal{C}
, a set
\mathrm{Ty}(\Gamma)
, called the set of all
types
in the
context

\Gamma
;

•

\mathrm{Tm}
assigns, to each pair
(\Gamma,A)
of an object
\Gamma\in\mathcal{C}
and a type
A\in\mathrm{Ty}(\Gamma)
, a set
\mathrm{Tm}(\Gamma,A)
, called the set of all
terms
of type
A
in the context
\Gamma
;

•

To each
f:\Delta\to\Gamma
in
\mathcal{C}
,
\_\{\_\}
assigns a map
\_\{f\}:\mathrm{Ty}(\Gamma)\to\mathrm{Ty}(\Delta)
, called the
substitution on types
, and a family
(\_\{f\}_{A})_{A\in\mathrm{Ty}(\Gamma)}
of maps
\_\{f\}_{A}:\mathrm{Tm}(\Gamma,A)\to\mathrm{Tm}(\Delta,A\{f\})
, called the
substitutions on terms
;

•

\_.\_
assigns, to each pair
(\Gamma,A)
of a context
\Gamma\in\mathcal{C}
and a type
A\in\mathrm{Ty}(\Gamma)
, a context
\Gamma.A\in\mathcal{C}
, called the
comprehension
of
A
;

•

\mathrm{p}
(resp.
\mathrm{v}
) associates each pair
(\Gamma,A)
of a context
\Gamma\in\mathcal{C}
and a type
A\in\mathrm{Ty}(\Gamma)
with a morphism
\mathrm{p}(A):\Gamma.A\to\Gamma
in
\mathcal{C}
(resp. a term
\mathrm{v}_{A}\in\mathrm{Tm}(\Gamma.A,A\{\mathrm{p}(A)\})
), called the
first projection
on
A
(resp. the
second projection
on
A
);

•

\langle\_,\_\rangle_{\_}
assigns, to each triple
(f,A,g)
of a morphism
f:\Delta\to\Gamma
in
\mathcal{C}
, a type
A\in\mathrm{Ty}(\Gamma)
and a term
g\in\mathrm{Tm}(\Delta,A\{f\})
, a morphism
\langle f,g\rangle_{A}:\Delta\to\Gamma.A

in
\mathcal{C}
, called the
extension
of
f
by
g

that satisfies

•

(Ty-Id)

A\{\mathrm{id}_{\Gamma}\}=A
;

•

(Ty-Comp)

A\{f\circ e\}=A\{f\}\{e\}
;

•

(Tm-Id)

h\{\mathrm{id}_{\Gamma}\}_{A}=h
;

•

(Tm-Comp)

h\{f\circ e\}_{A}=h\{f\}_{A}\{e\}_{A\{f\}}
;

•

(Cons-L)

\mathrm{p}(A)\circ\langle f,g\rangle_{A}=f
;

•

(Cons-R)

\mathrm{v}_{A}\{\langle f,g\rangle_{A}\}=g
;

•

(Cons-Nat)

\langle f,g\rangle_{A}\circ e=\langle f\circ e,g\{e\}_{A\{f\}}\rangle_{A}
;

•

(Cons-Id)

\langle\mathrm{p}(A),\mathrm{v}_{A}\rangle_{A}=\mathrm{id}_{\Gamma.A}

for any
\Gamma,\Delta,\Theta\in\mathcal{C}
,
A\in\mathrm{Ty}(\Gamma)
,
f:\Delta\to\Gamma
,
e:\Theta\to\Delta
,
h\in\mathrm{Tm}(\Gamma,A)
and
g\in\mathrm{Tm}(\Delta,A\{f\})
.

Roughly, judgements of MLTT are modeled in a CwF
\mathcal{C}
by

\displaystyle\mathsf{\vdash\Gamma\ ctx}

\displaystyle\mapsto\llbracket\mathsf{\Gamma}\rrbracket\in\mathcal{C};

\displaystyle\mathsf{\Gamma\vdash A\ type}

\displaystyle\mapsto\llbracket\mathsf{A}\rrbracket\in\mathrm{Ty}(\llbracket\mathsf{\Gamma}\rrbracket);

\displaystyle\mathsf{\Gamma\vdash a:A}

\displaystyle\mapsto\llbracket\mathsf{a}\rrbracket\in\mathrm{Tm}(\llbracket\mathsf{\Gamma}\rrbracket,\llbracket\mathsf{A}\rrbracket);

\displaystyle\mathsf{\vdash\Gamma=\Delta\ ctx}

\displaystyle\Rightarrow\llbracket\mathsf{\Gamma}\rrbracket=\llbracket\mathsf{\Delta}\rrbracket\in\mathcal{C};

\displaystyle\mathsf{\Gamma\vdash A=B\ type}

\displaystyle\Rightarrow\llbracket\mathsf{A}\rrbracket=\llbracket\mathsf{B}\rrbracket\in\mathrm{Ty}(\llbracket\mathsf{\Gamma}\rrbracket);

\displaystyle\mathsf{\Gamma\vdash a=a^{\prime}:A}

\displaystyle\Rightarrow\llbracket\mathsf{a}\rrbracket=\llbracket\mathsf{a^{\prime}}\rrbracket\in\mathrm{Tm}(\llbracket\mathsf{\Gamma}\rrbracket,\llbracket\mathsf{A}\rrbracket),

where
\llbracket\_\rrbracket
denotes the
semantic map
or
interpretation

[
20
]
.
Strictly speaking, the first three maps define an interpretation
\llbracket\_\rrbracket
of MLTT in
\mathcal{C}
, while the last three logical implications are
soundness
of the interpretation
\llbracket\_\rrbracket
.
See
[
20
]
for the details.

Let us now turn to introducing our CwF of
realizability à la game semantics
:

Definition 3.38

(CwF of realizability à la game semantics)
.

The CwF
\mathbb{NPG}_{\mathrm{wrw}}^{\mathrm{wo}}
is the tuple
(\mathbb{NPG}_{\mathrm{wrw}}^{\mathrm{wo}},\mathrm{Ty},\mathrm{Tm},\_\{\_\},\bm{1},\_.\_,\mathrm{p},\mathrm{v},\langle\_,\_\rangle_{\_})
,
where

•

The category
\mathbb{NPG}_{\mathrm{wrw}}^{\mathrm{wo}}
is the one defined in Definition
3.26
, and
\bm{1}\in\mathbb{NPG}_{\mathrm{wrw}}^{\mathrm{wo}}
is the unit np-game (Example
3.9
);

•

Given
\Gamma\in\mathbb{NPG}_{\mathrm{wrw}}^{\mathrm{wo}}
and
A\in\mathscr{D}\mathbb{NPG}_{\mathrm{wrw}}^{\mathrm{wo}}(\Gamma)
, we define
\mathrm{Ty}(\Gamma)\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\mathscr{D}\mathbb{NPG}_{\mathrm{wrw}}^{\mathrm{wo}}(\Gamma)
and
\mathrm{Tm}(\Gamma,A)\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\mathcal{TS}_{\mathrm{wr}}(\Pi_{\mathrm{wrw}}(\Gamma,A))
;

•

Given
\uplus\phi:\Delta\rightarrow\Gamma
in
\mathbb{NPG}_{\mathrm{wrw}}^{\mathrm{wo}}
, we define the map
\_\{\uplus\phi\}:\mathrm{Ty}(\Gamma)\to\mathrm{Ty}(\Delta)
by

A\{\uplus\phi\}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}(A(\uplus\phi\bullet\uplus\delta))_{\uplus\delta\in\mathbb{NPG}_{\mathrm{wrw}}^{\mathrm{wo}}(\Delta)}\quad(A\in\mathrm{Ty}(\Gamma)),

and the map
\_\{\uplus\phi\}_{A}:\mathrm{Tm}(\Gamma,A)\to\mathrm{Tm}(\Delta,A\{\uplus\phi\})
by

\uplus\alpha\{\uplus\phi\}_{A}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\uplus\alpha\bullet\uplus\phi\quad(\uplus\alpha\in\mathrm{Tm}(\Gamma,A));

•

The comprehension
\Gamma.A
is given by
\Gamma.A\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\Sigma_{\mathrm{wrw}}(\Gamma,A)
, the first projection
\mathrm{p}(A)\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\mathrm{fst}_{\Sigma_{\mathrm{wrw}}(\Gamma,A)}:\Sigma_{\mathrm{wrw}}(\Gamma,A)\to\Gamma
is the w.r.w. dereliction
\uplus\mathrm{der}_{\Gamma}
up to ‘tags,’ the second projection
\mathrm{v}_{A}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\mathrm{snd}_{\Sigma_{\mathrm{wrw}}(\Gamma,A)}\in\mathrm{Tm}(\Sigma_{\mathrm{wrw}}(\Gamma,A),A\{\mathrm{p}(A)\})
is the w.r.w. dereliction
\uplus\mathrm{der}_{\int_{\Gamma}A}
up to ‘tags,’ and the extension
\langle\uplus\phi,\uplus\alpha\rangle_{A}:\Delta\rightarrow\Sigma_{\mathrm{wrw}}(\Gamma,A)
is the pairing
\langle\uplus\phi,\uplus\alpha\rangle
of the DoWRWLIs
\uplus\phi
and
\uplus\alpha
.

Convention
.

We frequently omit the subscript
(\_)_{A}
on
\_\{\_\}_{A}
and
\langle\_,\_\rangle_{A}
, and the one
(\_)_{\Sigma_{\mathrm{wrw}}(\Gamma,A)}
on
\mathrm{fst}_{\Sigma_{\mathrm{wrw}}(\Gamma,A)}
and
\mathrm{snd}_{\Sigma_{\mathrm{wrw}}(\Gamma,A)}
.

Theorem 3.39

(Well-defined CwF of realizability à la game semantics)
.

The structure
\mathbb{NPG}_{\mathrm{wrw}}^{\mathrm{wo}}
given in Definition
3.38
forms a well-defined CwF.

Proof.

We focus on substitutions on terms and extensions as other verifications are straightforward.
Let
\Gamma,\Delta\in\mathbb{NPG}_{\mathrm{wrw}}^{\mathrm{wo}}
,
A\in\mathscr{D}\mathbb{NPG}_{\mathrm{wrw}}^{\mathrm{wo}}(\Gamma)
,
\uplus\phi\in\mathbb{NPG}_{\mathrm{wrw}}^{\mathrm{wo}}(\Delta,\Gamma)
,
\uplus\alpha\in\mathcal{TS}_{\mathrm{wr}}(\Pi_{\mathrm{wrw}}(\Gamma,A))
and
\uplus\tilde{\alpha}\in\mathcal{TS}_{\mathrm{wr}}(\Pi_{\mathrm{wrw}}(\Delta,A\{\uplus\phi\}))
, and assume that
\mathscr{R}_{\mathrm{wr}}^{\mathrm{cp}}(\Gamma)\neq\emptyset
and
\mathscr{R}_{\mathrm{wr}}^{\mathrm{cp}}(\Delta)\neq\emptyset
, following our convention, since the other cases are trivial.

By Lemma
3.12
,
\uplus\alpha\{\uplus\phi\}=\uplus\alpha\bullet\uplus\phi
is a winning, recursive t-skeleton on
\hat{\oc}\Delta\rightarrowtriangle\int_{\Gamma}A
.
Take any
\uplus\delta\in\mathbb{NPG}_{\mathrm{wrw}}^{\mathrm{wo}}(\Delta)
; for proving
\uplus\alpha\{\uplus\phi\}\in\mathcal{TS}_{\mathrm{wr}}(\Pi_{\mathrm{wrw}}(\Delta,A\{\uplus\phi\}))
, it remains to show
(\uplus\alpha\bullet\uplus\phi)\bullet\uplus\delta::\oc\bm{1}\rightarrowtriangle A\{\uplus\phi\}(\uplus\delta)
.
Then, we calculate

\displaystyle(\uplus\alpha\bullet\uplus\phi)\bullet\uplus\delta

\displaystyle=(\uplus\alpha\circ\uplus\phi^{\dagger})\circ\uplus\delta^{\dagger}

\displaystyle=\uplus\alpha\circ(\uplus\phi\circ\uplus\delta^{\dagger})^{\dagger}\quad\text{(by Lemma~\ref{LemWRWPromotionLemma})}

\displaystyle=\uplus\alpha\circ(\uplus\phi\bullet\uplus\delta)^{\dagger}::\oc\bm{1}\rightarrowtriangle A^{\ddagger}((\uplus\phi\bullet\uplus\delta)^{\dagger}),

where

\displaystyle A^{\ddagger}((\uplus\phi\bullet\uplus\delta)^{\dagger})

\displaystyle=A(\uplus\phi\bullet\uplus\delta)

\displaystyle=A\{\uplus\phi\}(\uplus\delta).

Hence, we have shown that
\uplus\alpha\{\uplus\phi\}\in\mathcal{TS}_{\mathrm{wr}}(\Pi_{\mathrm{wrw}}(\Delta,A\{\uplus\phi\}))
holds.

Similarly, the extension
\langle\uplus\phi,\uplus\tilde{\alpha}\rangle
is a winning, recursive t-skeleton on
\hat{\oc}\Delta\rightarrowtriangle\Gamma\mathbin{\&}(\int_{\Gamma}A)
by Lemma
3.24
.
Hence, for proving
\langle\uplus\phi,\uplus\tilde{\alpha}\rangle\in\mathbb{NPG}_{\mathrm{wrw}}^{\mathrm{wo}}(\Delta,\Sigma_{\mathrm{wrw}}(\Gamma,A))
, it suffices to show
\uplus\tilde{\alpha}\bullet\uplus\delta::\oc\bm{1}\rightarrowtriangle A(\uplus\phi\bullet\uplus\delta)
since we have
\langle\uplus\phi,\uplus\tilde{\alpha}\rangle\bullet\uplus\delta=\langle\uplus\phi\bullet\uplus\delta,\uplus\tilde{\alpha}\bullet\uplus\delta\rangle
with
\uplus\phi\bullet\uplus\delta
winning and recursive.
Then, we calculate

\uplus\tilde{\alpha}\bullet\uplus\delta::\oc\bm{1}\rightarrowtriangle A\{\uplus\phi\}(\uplus\delta)=\oc\bm{1}\rightarrowtriangle A(\uplus\phi\bullet\uplus\delta).

Finally, let us verify the required equations:

•

(Ty-Id)

A\{\mathrm{id}_{\Gamma}\}=(A(\uplus\mathrm{der}_{\Gamma}\bullet\uplus\gamma))_{\uplus\gamma\in\mathbb{NPG}_{\mathrm{wrw}}^{\mathrm{wo}}(\Gamma)}=(A(\uplus\gamma))_{\uplus\gamma\in\mathbb{NPG}_{\mathrm{wrw}}^{\mathrm{wo}}(\Gamma)}=A
by Lemma
3.25
;

•

(Ty-Comp)
Given
\Theta\in\mathbb{NPG}_{\mathrm{wrw}}^{\mathrm{wo}}
and
\uplus\psi:\Gamma\rightarrow\Theta
in
\mathbb{NPG}_{\mathrm{wrw}}^{\mathrm{wo}}
, we calculate

\displaystyle A\{\uplus\psi\bullet\uplus\phi\}

\displaystyle=(A((\uplus\psi\bullet\uplus\phi)\bullet\uplus\delta))_{\uplus\delta\in\mathbb{NPG}_{\mathrm{wrw}}^{\mathrm{wo}}(\Delta)}

\displaystyle=(A(\uplus\psi\bullet(\uplus\phi\bullet\uplus\delta)))_{\uplus\delta\in\mathbb{NPG}_{\mathrm{wrw}}^{\mathrm{wo}}(\Delta)}\quad\text{(by Lemma~\ref{LemWRWPromotionLemma})}

\displaystyle=(A\{\uplus\psi\}(\uplus\phi\bullet\uplus\delta))_{\uplus\delta\in\mathbb{NPG}_{\mathrm{wrw}}^{\mathrm{wo}}(\Delta)}

\displaystyle=(A\{\uplus\psi\}\{\uplus\phi\}(\uplus\delta))_{\uplus\delta\in\mathbb{NPG}_{\mathrm{wrw}}^{\mathrm{wo}}(\Delta)}

\displaystyle=A\{\uplus\psi\}\{\uplus\phi\};

•

(Tm-Id)

\uplus\alpha\{\mathrm{id}_{\Gamma}\}=\uplus\alpha\bullet\uplus\mathrm{der}_{\Gamma}=\uplus\alpha\circ\uplus\mathrm{cp}_{\hat{\oc}\Gamma}=\uplus\alpha
by Lemmata
3.22
and
3.25
;

•

(Tm-Comp)

\uplus\alpha\{\uplus\psi\bullet\uplus\phi\}=\uplus\alpha\bullet(\uplus\psi\bullet\uplus\phi)=(\uplus\alpha\bullet\uplus\psi)\bullet\uplus\phi=\uplus\alpha\{\uplus\psi\}\bullet\uplus\phi=\uplus\alpha\{\uplus\psi\}\{\uplus\phi\}
by Lemma
3.25
;

•

(Cons-L)

\mathrm{p}(A)\bullet\langle\uplus\psi,\uplus\tilde{\alpha}\rangle=\mathrm{fst}\circ\langle\uplus\psi,\uplus\tilde{\alpha}\rangle^{\dagger}=\mathrm{fst}\circ\langle\uplus\psi^{\dagger},\uplus\tilde{\alpha}^{\dagger}\rangle=\uplus\psi
;

•

(Cons-R)

\mathrm{v}_{A}\{\langle\uplus\psi,\uplus\tilde{\alpha}\rangle\}=\mathrm{snd}\circ\langle\uplus\psi,\uplus\tilde{\alpha}\rangle^{\dagger}=\mathrm{snd}\circ\langle\uplus\psi^{\dagger},\uplus\tilde{\alpha}^{\dagger}\rangle=\uplus\tilde{\alpha}
;

•

(Cons-Nat)

\langle\uplus\psi,\uplus\tilde{\alpha}\rangle\bullet\uplus\phi=\langle\uplus\psi\bullet\uplus\phi,\uplus\tilde{\alpha}\bullet\uplus\phi\rangle=\langle\uplus\psi\bullet\uplus\phi,\uplus\tilde{\alpha}\{\uplus\phi\}\rangle
;

•

(Cons-Id)

\langle\mathrm{p}(A),\mathrm{v}_{A}\rangle=\langle\mathrm{fst}_{\Sigma_{\mathrm{wrw}}(\Gamma,A)},\mathrm{snd}_{\Sigma_{\mathrm{wrw}}(\Gamma,A)}\rangle=\uplus\mathrm{der}_{\Sigma_{\mathrm{wrw}}(\Gamma,A)}=\mathrm{id}_{\Gamma.A}
,

which completes the proof.
∎

Let us then proceed to equip the CwF
\mathbb{NPG}_{\mathrm{wrw}}^{\mathrm{wo}}
with semantic type formers for unit-, empty-, N-, pi-, sigma- and Id-types.
We begin with
pi-types
(Appendix
A.6
).
Recall first the semantic type former for pi-types in an arbitrary CwF:

Definition 3.40

(CwFs with pi-types
[
20
]
)
.

A CwF
\mathcal{C}

supports pi
if

•

(
\Pi
-Form)
Given
\Gamma\in\mathcal{C}
,
A\in\mathrm{Ty}(\Gamma)
and
B\in\mathrm{Ty}(\Gamma.A)
, there is a type
\Pi(A,B)\in\mathrm{Ty}(\Gamma)
;

•

(
\Pi
-Intro)
Given
b\in\mathrm{Tm}(\Gamma.A,B)
, there is a term
\lambda_{A,B}(b)\in\mathrm{Tm}(\Gamma,\Pi(A,B))
;

•

(
\Pi
-Elim)
Given
k\in\mathrm{Tm}(\Gamma,\Pi(A,B))
and
a\in\mathrm{Tm}(\Gamma,A)
, there is a term
\mathrm{App}_{A,B}(k,a)\in\mathrm{Tm}(\Gamma,B\{\overline{a}\})
, where
\overline{a}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\langle\mathrm{id}_{\Gamma},a\rangle_{A}:\Gamma\to\Gamma.A
;

•

(
\Pi
-Comp)

\mathrm{App}_{A,B}(\lambda_{A,B}(b),a)=b\{\overline{a}\}
;

•

(
\Pi
-Subst)
Given
\Delta\in\mathcal{C}
and
f:\Delta\to\Gamma
in
\mathcal{C}
,
\Pi(A,B)\{f\}=\Pi(A\{f\},B\{f^{+}\})
, where
f^{+}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\langle f\circ\mathrm{p}(A\{f\}),\mathrm{v}_{A\{f\}}\rangle_{A}:\Delta.A\{f\}\to\Gamma.A
;

•

(
\lambda
-Subst)

\lambda_{A,B}(b)\{f\}=\lambda_{A\{f\},B\{f^{+}\}}(b\{f^{+}\})\in\mathrm{Tm}(\Delta,\Pi(A\{f\},B\{f^{+}\}))
;

•

(App-Subst)

\mathrm{App}_{A,B}(k,a)\{f\}=\mathrm{App}_{A\{f\},B\{f^{+}\}}(k\{f\},a\{f\})\in\mathrm{Tm}(\Delta,B\{\overline{a}\}\{f\})
.

Furthermore,
\mathcal{C}

supports pi in the strict sense
if it additionally satisfies

•

(
\lambda
-Uniq)

\lambda_{A,B}\circ\mathrm{App}_{A\{\mathrm{p}(A)\},B\{\mathrm{p}(A)^{+}\}}(k\{\mathrm{p}(A)\},\mathrm{v}_{A})=k
.

Pi-types (with the
\eta
-rule) are modeled in a CwF that supports pi (in the strict sense); see Appendix
A.6
for the details.

Let us now show that the CwF
\mathbb{NPG}_{\mathrm{wrw}}^{\mathrm{wo}}
supports pi in the strict sense:

Lemma 3.41

(Winning-realizer-wise currying lemma)
.

Given
\Gamma\in\mathbb{NPG}_{\mathrm{wrw}}^{\mathrm{wo}}
,
A\in\mathscr{D}\mathbb{NPG}_{\mathrm{wrw}}^{\mathrm{wo}}(\Gamma)
and
B\in\mathscr{D}\mathbb{NPG}_{\mathrm{wrw}}^{\mathrm{wo}}(\Sigma_{\mathrm{wrw}}(\Gamma,A))
, there is a bijection

\lambda_{A,B}:\mathcal{TS}_{\mathrm{wr}}(\Pi_{\mathrm{wrw}}(\Sigma_{\mathrm{wrw}}(\Gamma,A),B))\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\rightarrow}}\mathcal{TS}_{\mathrm{wr}}(\Pi_{\mathrm{wrw}}(\Gamma,\Pi_{\mathrm{wrw}}(A,B))),

where
\Pi_{\mathrm{wrw}}(A,B)\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}(\Pi_{\mathrm{wrw}}(A(\uplus\gamma),B_{\uplus\gamma}))_{\uplus\gamma\in\mathbb{NPG}_{\mathrm{wrw}}^{\mathrm{wo}}(\Gamma)}
and
B_{\uplus\gamma}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}(B(\langle\uplus\gamma,\uplus\alpha\rangle)_{\uplus\alpha\in\mathbb{NPG}_{\mathrm{wrw}}^{\mathrm{wo}}(A(\uplus\gamma))}
.

Proof.

It suffices to show that there is a bijection between (not necessarily winning or recursive) t-skeletons on
\Pi_{\mathrm{wrw}}(\Sigma_{\mathrm{wrw}}(\Gamma,A),B)
and those on
\Pi_{\mathrm{wrw}}(\Gamma,\Pi_{\mathrm{wrw}}(A,B))
up to finitary ‘tags’ since such a bijection preserves winning and recursiveness.

Recall that a t-skeleton
\uplus\phi::\Pi_{\mathrm{wrw}}(\Sigma_{\mathrm{wrw}}(\Gamma,A),B)
is the disjoint union on a family
\phi=(\phi_{(\sigma^{\dagger},e)})_{(\sigma^{\dagger},e)\in\mathscr{R}^{\mathrm{cp}}_{\mathrm{wr}}(\hat{\oc}\Sigma_{\mathrm{wrw}}(\Gamma,A))}
of t-skeletons
\phi_{(\sigma^{\dagger},e)}::\sigma^{\dagger}\rightarrowtriangle\mathrm{cod}_{\phi}(\sigma^{\dagger},e)
such that
\mathrm{cod}_{\phi}(\sigma^{\dagger},e)\in\mathcal{TS}_{\mathrm{wr}}(B(\uplus\{\sigma^{\hat{\oc}\bm{1}}\}))
, each
\phi_{(\sigma^{\dagger},e)}
does not depend on
\sigma^{\dagger}
, and the realizer-map
\pi_{\phi}
is ‘effective’ (Definitions
3.10
,
3.11
,
3.30
and
3.31
).

Similarly, a t-skeleton
\uplus\psi::\Pi_{\mathrm{wrw}}(\Gamma,\Pi_{\mathrm{wrw}}(A,B))
is the disjoint union on a family
\psi=(\psi_{(\gamma^{\dagger},f)})_{(\gamma^{\dagger},f)\in\mathscr{R}^{\mathrm{cp}}_{\mathrm{wr}}(\hat{\oc}\Gamma)}
of t-skeletons
\psi_{(\gamma^{\dagger},f)}::\gamma^{\dagger}\rightarrowtriangle\mathrm{cod}_{\psi}(\gamma^{\dagger},f)
such that
\mathrm{cod}_{\psi}(\gamma^{\dagger},f)\in\mathcal{TS}_{\mathrm{wr}}(\Pi_{\mathrm{wrw}}(A,B)(\uplus\{\gamma^{\hat{\oc}\bm{1}}\}))
, each
\psi_{(\gamma^{\dagger},f)}
does not depend on
\gamma^{\dagger}
, and the realizer-map
\pi_{\psi}
is ‘effective.’
Note that for each
\sigma_{0}^{\dagger}\in\mathcal{TS}_{\mathrm{wr}}(\hat{\oc}\Sigma(\Gamma,A))
we may write
\sigma_{0}^{\dagger}=\langle\gamma_{0}^{\dagger},\alpha_{0}^{\dagger}\rangle
for some
\gamma_{0}\in\mathcal{TS}_{\mathrm{wr}}(\Gamma)
and
\alpha_{0}\in\mathcal{TS}_{\mathrm{wr}}(A(\uplus\{\gamma_{0}^{\hat{\oc}\bm{1}}\}))
.

Then, by applying the
currying
on skeletons in game semantics
[
33
,
36
]
, which simply adjusts the finitary ‘tags’ for product and implication appropriately, to each component
\phi_{(\sigma^{\dagger},e)}
of a t-skeleton
\uplus\phi::\Pi_{\mathrm{wrw}}(\Sigma_{\mathrm{wrw}}(\Gamma,A),B)
in the evident way, it is easy to see that we get a t-skeleton
\lambda_{A,B}(\uplus\phi)::\Pi_{\mathrm{wrw}}(\Gamma,\Pi_{\mathrm{wrw}}(A,B))
.
Finally, this operation on t-skeletons has the evident inverse, which completes the proof.
∎

Remark
.

The proof of Lemma
3.41
is essentially the same as the corresponding one given in the previous work
[
35
]
, and thus it explains why our realizability à la game semantics validates the
\xi
-rule just like the existing game semantics does.

Theorem 3.42

(Realizability model of pi-types à la game semantics)
.

The CwF
\mathbb{NPG}_{\mathrm{wrw}}^{\mathrm{wo}}
supports pi in the strict sense.

Proof.

Let
\Gamma\in\mathbb{NPG}_{\mathrm{wrw}}^{\mathrm{wo}}
,
A\in\mathscr{D}\mathbb{NPG}_{\mathrm{wrw}}^{\mathrm{wo}}(\Gamma)
,
B\in\mathscr{D}\mathbb{NPG}_{\mathrm{wrw}}^{\mathrm{wo}}(\Sigma_{\mathrm{wrw}}(\Gamma,A))
and
\uplus\beta\in\mathcal{TS}_{\mathrm{wr}}(\Pi_{\mathrm{wrw}}(\Sigma_{\mathrm{wrw}}(\Gamma,A),B))
.

•

(
\Pi
-Form)
Let us define
\Pi(A,B)\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}(\Pi_{\mathrm{wrw}}(A(\uplus\gamma),B_{\uplus\gamma}))_{\uplus\gamma\in\mathbb{NPG}_{\mathrm{wrw}}^{\mathrm{wo}}(\Gamma)}
, where
B_{\uplus\gamma}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}(B(\langle\uplus\gamma,\uplus\tilde{\alpha}\rangle)_{\uplus\tilde{\alpha}\in\mathbb{NPG}_{\mathrm{wrw}}^{\mathrm{wo}}(A(\uplus\gamma))}\in\mathscr{D}\mathbb{NPG}_{\mathrm{wrw}}^{\mathrm{wo}}(A(\uplus\gamma))
.
To distinguish it from the game semantics of pi-types in
[
35
]
, we write
\Pi_{\mathrm{wrw}}(A,B)
for
\Pi(A,B)
.

•

(
\Pi
-Intro)
We get
\lambda_{A,B}(\uplus\beta)\in\mathcal{TS}_{\mathrm{wr}}(\Pi_{\mathrm{wrw}}(\Gamma,\Pi_{\mathrm{wrw}}(A,B)))
by Lemma
3.41
, where recall that
\lambda_{A,B}
and
\lambda_{A,B}^{-1}
simply ‘adjust finitary tags’ on standard moves.
We often omit the subscripts
(\_)_{A,B}
on
\lambda_{A,B}
and
\lambda_{A,B}^{-1}
.

•

(
\Pi
-Elim)
Given
\uplus\kappa\in\mathcal{TS}_{\mathrm{wr}}(\Pi_{\mathrm{wrw}}(\Gamma,\Pi_{\mathrm{wrw}}(A,B)))
and
\uplus\alpha\in\mathcal{TS}_{\mathrm{wr}}(\Pi_{\mathrm{wrw}}(\Gamma,A))
, we define
\mathrm{App}_{A,B}(\uplus\kappa,\uplus\alpha)\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\lambda_{A,B}^{-1}(\uplus\kappa)\bullet\overline{\uplus\alpha}
.
As in the proof of Theorem
3.39
,
\lambda_{A,B}^{-1}(\uplus\kappa)\bullet\overline{\uplus\alpha}::\Pi_{\mathrm{wrw}}(\Gamma,B\{\overline{\uplus\alpha}\})
, and so
\mathrm{App}_{A,B}(\uplus\kappa,\uplus\alpha)=\lambda_{A,B}^{-1}(\uplus\kappa)\bullet\overline{\uplus\alpha}\in\mathcal{TS}_{\mathrm{wr}}(\Pi_{\mathrm{wrw}}(\Gamma,B\{\overline{\uplus\alpha}\}))
.
We often omit the subscripts
A,B
on
\mathrm{App}_{A,B}
.

•

(
\Pi
-Comp)

\mathrm{App}(\lambda(\uplus\beta),\uplus\alpha)=\lambda_{A,B}^{-1}(\lambda_{A,B}(\uplus\beta))\bullet\overline{\uplus\alpha}=\uplus\beta\bullet\overline{\uplus\alpha}=\uplus\beta\{\overline{\uplus\alpha}\}
.

•

(
\Pi
-Subst)
Given
\Delta\in\mathbb{NPG}_{\mathrm{wrw}}^{\mathrm{wo}}
and
\uplus\phi\in\mathbb{NPG}_{\mathrm{wrw}}^{\mathrm{wo}}(\Delta,\Gamma)
, we calculate

\displaystyle\textstyle\Pi_{\mathrm{wrw}}(A,B)\{\uplus\phi\}

\displaystyle=(\Pi_{\mathrm{wrw}}(A(\uplus\gamma),B_{\uplus\gamma}))_{\uplus\gamma\in\mathbb{NPG}_{\mathrm{wrw}}^{\mathrm{wo}}(\Gamma)}\{\uplus\phi\}

\displaystyle=(\Pi_{\mathrm{wrw}}(A(\uplus\phi\bullet\uplus\delta),B_{\uplus\phi\bullet\uplus\delta}))_{\uplus\delta\in\mathbb{NPG}_{\mathrm{wrw}}^{\mathrm{wo}}(\Delta)}

\displaystyle=(\Pi_{\mathrm{wrw}}(A\{\uplus\phi\}(\uplus\delta),B\{\uplus\phi^{+}\}_{\uplus\delta}))_{\uplus\delta\in\mathbb{NPG}_{\mathrm{wrw}}^{\mathrm{wo}}(\Delta)}

\displaystyle=\Pi_{\mathrm{wrw}}(A\{\uplus\phi\},B\{\uplus\phi^{+}\}),

where
B\{\uplus\phi^{+}\}_{\uplus\delta}=B_{\uplus\phi\bullet\uplus\delta}
since for all
\uplus\alpha^{\prime}\in\mathbb{NPG}_{\mathrm{wrw}}^{\mathrm{wo}}(A(\uplus\phi\bullet\uplus\delta))
we have

\displaystyle B\{\uplus\phi^{+}\}_{\uplus\delta}(\uplus\alpha^{\prime})

\displaystyle=B\{\uplus\phi^{+}\}(\langle\uplus\delta,\uplus\alpha^{\prime}\rangle)

\displaystyle=B(\langle\uplus\phi\bullet\mathrm{p}(A\{\uplus\phi\}),\mathrm{v}_{A\{\uplus\phi\}}\rangle\bullet\langle\uplus\delta,\uplus\alpha^{\prime}\rangle)

\displaystyle=B(\langle\uplus\phi\bullet\uplus\delta,\uplus\alpha^{\prime}\rangle)

\displaystyle=B_{\uplus\phi\bullet\uplus\delta}(\uplus\alpha^{\prime}).

•

(
\lambda
-Subst)
We calculate

\displaystyle\lambda(\uplus\beta)\{\uplus\phi\}

\displaystyle=\lambda_{A,B}(\uplus\beta)\bullet\uplus\phi

\displaystyle=\lambda_{A\{\uplus\phi\},B\{\uplus\phi^{+}\}}(\uplus\beta\bullet\langle\uplus\phi\bullet\mathrm{fst},\mathrm{snd}\rangle)\quad\text{(by the definition of $\lambda$)}

\displaystyle=\lambda_{A\{\uplus\phi\},B\{\uplus\phi^{+}\}}(\uplus\beta\bullet\langle\uplus\phi\bullet\mathrm{p}(A\{\uplus\phi\}),\mathrm{v}_{A\{\uplus\phi\}}\rangle)

\displaystyle=\lambda_{A\{\uplus\phi\},B\{\uplus\phi^{+}\}}(\uplus\beta\{\uplus\phi^{+}\}).

•

(App-Subst)
We calculate

\displaystyle\mathrm{App}(\uplus\kappa,\uplus\alpha)\{\uplus\phi\}

\displaystyle=(\lambda_{A,B}^{-1}(\uplus\kappa)\bullet\langle\uplus\mathrm{der}_{\Gamma},\uplus\alpha\rangle)\bullet\uplus\phi

\displaystyle=\lambda_{A,B}^{-1}(\uplus\kappa)\bullet(\langle\uplus\mathrm{der}_{\Gamma},\uplus\alpha\rangle\bullet\uplus\phi)

\displaystyle=\lambda_{A,B}^{-1}(\uplus\kappa)\bullet\langle\uplus\phi,\uplus\alpha\bullet\uplus\phi\rangle

\displaystyle=\lambda_{A,B}^{-1}(\uplus\kappa)\bullet(\langle\uplus\phi\bullet\mathrm{p}(A\{\uplus\phi\}),\mathrm{v}_{A\{\uplus\phi\}}\rangle\bullet\langle\uplus\mathrm{der}_{\Delta},\uplus\alpha\bullet\uplus\phi\rangle)

\displaystyle=(\lambda_{A,B}^{-1}(\uplus\kappa)\bullet\uplus\phi^{+})\bullet\overline{(\uplus\alpha\bullet\uplus\phi)}

\displaystyle=\lambda_{A\{\uplus\phi\},B\{\uplus\phi^{+}\}}^{-1}(\uplus\kappa\bullet\uplus\phi)\bullet\overline{(\uplus\alpha\bullet\uplus\phi)}\quad\text{(by $\lambda$-Subst)}

\displaystyle=\lambda_{A\{\uplus\phi\},B\{\uplus\phi^{+}\}}^{-1}(\uplus\kappa\bullet\uplus\phi)\{\overline{(\uplus\alpha\bullet\uplus\phi)}\}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{App}_{A\{\uplus\phi\},B\{\uplus\phi^{+}\}}(\uplus\kappa\bullet\uplus\phi,\uplus\alpha\bullet\uplus\phi)\quad\text{(by $\Pi$-Comp)}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{App}_{A\{\uplus\phi\},B\{\uplus\phi^{+}\}}(\uplus\kappa\{\uplus\phi\},\uplus\alpha\{\uplus\phi\}).

•

(
\lambda
-Uniq)
Finally, we calculate

\displaystyle\lambda(\mathrm{App}(\uplus\kappa\{\mathrm{p}(A)\},\mathrm{v}_{A}))

\displaystyle=\lambda_{A,B}(\lambda^{-1}_{A\{\mathrm{p}(A)\},B\{\mathrm{p}(A)^{+}\}}(\uplus\kappa\{\mathrm{p}(A)\})\bullet\overline{\mathrm{v}_{A}})

\displaystyle=\lambda_{A,B}((\lambda^{-1}_{A,B}(\uplus\kappa)\bullet\mathrm{p}(A)^{+})\bullet\overline{\mathrm{v}_{A}})\quad\text{(by $\lambda$-Subst)}

\displaystyle=\lambda_{A,B}(\lambda^{-1}_{A,B}(\uplus\kappa)\bullet(\mathrm{p}(A)^{+}\bullet\overline{\mathrm{v}_{A}}))

\displaystyle=\lambda_{A,B}(\lambda^{-1}_{A,B}(\uplus\kappa)\bullet\uplus\mathrm{der}_{\Sigma_{\mathrm{wrw}}(\Gamma,A)})

\displaystyle=\lambda_{A,B}(\lambda^{-1}_{A,B}(\uplus\kappa))

\displaystyle=\uplus\kappa,

which completes the proof.
∎

Next, we consider
sigma-types
(Appendix
A.7
).
Again, we first recall the semantic type former for sigma-types in an arbitrary CwF:

Definition 3.43

(CwFs with sigma-types
[
20
]
)
.

A CwF
\mathcal{C}

supports sigma
if

•

(
\Sigma
-Form)
Given
\Gamma\in\mathcal{C}
,
A\in\mathrm{Ty}(\Gamma)
and
B\in\mathrm{Ty}(\Gamma.A)
, there is a type
\Sigma(A,B)\in\mathrm{Ty}(\Gamma)
;

•

(
\Sigma
-Intro)
There is a morphism
\mathrm{Pair}_{A,B}:\Gamma.A.B\to\Gamma.\Sigma(A,B)
in
\mathcal{C}
;

•

(
\Sigma
-Elim)
Given
P\in\mathrm{Ty}(\Gamma.\Sigma(A,B))
and
p\in\mathrm{Tm}(\Gamma.A.B,P\{\mathrm{Pair}_{A,B}\})
, there is a term
\mathcal{R}^{\Sigma}_{A,B,P}(p)\in\mathrm{Tm}(\Gamma.\Sigma(A,B),P)
;

•

(
\Sigma
-Comp)

\mathcal{R}^{\Sigma}_{A,B,P}(p)\{\mathrm{Pair}_{A,B}\}=p
;

•

(
\Sigma
-Subst)
Given
\Delta\in\mathcal{C}
and
f:\Delta\to\Gamma
in
\mathcal{C}
,
\Sigma(A,B)\{f\}=\Sigma(A\{f\},B\{f^{+}\})
, where
f^{+}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\langle f\circ\mathrm{p}(A\{f\}),\mathrm{v}_{A\{f\}}\rangle_{A}:\Delta.A\{f\}\to\Gamma.A
;

•

(Pair-Subst)

\mathrm{p}(\Sigma(A,B))\circ\mathrm{Pair}_{A,B}=\mathrm{p}(A)\circ\mathrm{p}(B)
and
f^{\star}\circ\mathrm{Pair}_{A\{f\},B\{f^{+}\}}=\mathrm{Pair}_{A,B}\circ f^{++}
, where

f^{\star}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\langle f\circ\mathrm{p}(\Sigma(A,B)\{f\}),\mathrm{v}_{\Sigma(A,B)\{f\}}\rangle_{\Sigma(A,B)}:\Delta.\Sigma(A,B)\{f\}\to\Gamma.\Sigma(A,B);\and f^{++}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\langle f^{+}\circ\mathrm{p}(B\{f^{+}\}),\mathrm{v}_{B\{f^{+}\}}\rangle_{B}:\Delta.A\{f\}.B\{f^{+}\}\to\Gamma.A.B;

•

(
\mathcal{R}^{\Sigma}
-Subst)

\mathcal{R}^{\Sigma}_{A,B,P}(p)\{f^{\star}\}=\mathcal{R}^{\Sigma}_{A\{f\},B\{f^{+}\},P\{f^{\star}\}}(p\{f^{++}\})
.

Moreover,
\mathcal{C}

supports sigma in the strict sense
if it also satisfies

•

(
\mathcal{R}^{\Sigma}
-Uniq)
If
p\in\mathrm{Tm}(\Gamma.A.B,P\{\mathrm{Pair}_{A,B}\})
,
q\in\mathrm{Tm}(\Gamma.\Sigma(A,B),P)
and
q\{\mathrm{Pair}_{A,B}\}=p
, then
q=\mathcal{R}^{\Sigma}_{A,B,P}(p)
.

Sigma-types (with the
\eta
-rule) are modeled in a CwF that supports sigma (in the strict sense); see Appendix
A.7
for the details.

Now, let us describe our interpretation of sigma-types:

Theorem 3.44

(Realizability model of sigma-types à la game semantics)
.

The CwF
\mathbb{NPG}_{\mathrm{wrw}}^{\mathrm{wo}}
supports sigma in the strict sense.

Proof.

Let
\Gamma,\Delta\in\mathbb{NPG}_{\mathrm{wrw}}^{\mathrm{wo}}
,
\uplus\phi\in\mathbb{NPG}_{\mathrm{wrw}}^{\mathrm{wo}}(\Delta,\Gamma)
,
A\in\mathscr{D}\mathbb{NPG}_{\mathrm{wrw}}^{\mathrm{wo}}(\Gamma)
and
B\in\mathscr{D}\mathbb{NPG}_{\mathrm{wrw}}^{\mathrm{wo}}(\Sigma_{\mathrm{wrw}}(\Gamma,A))
.

•

(
\Sigma
-Form)
Similarly to pi, let
\Sigma(A,B)\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}(\Sigma_{\mathrm{wrw}}(A(\uplus\gamma),B_{\uplus\gamma}))_{\uplus\gamma\in\mathbb{NPG}_{\mathrm{wrw}}^{\mathrm{wo}}(\Gamma)}
.
Again, to distinguish it from the game semantics of sigma-types given in the previous work
[
35
]
, we write
\Sigma_{\mathrm{wrw}}(A,B)
for
\Sigma(A,B)
.

•

(
\Sigma
-Intro)
By the isomorphism
\Sigma_{\mathrm{wrw}}(\Sigma_{\mathrm{wrw}}(\Gamma,A),B)\cong\Sigma_{\mathrm{wrw}}(\Gamma,\Sigma_{\mathrm{wrw}}(A,B))
, which is similar to (and simpler than) the one in Lemma
3.41
and left to the reader, let
\mathrm{Pair}_{A,B}:\Sigma_{\mathrm{wrw}}(\Sigma_{\mathrm{wrw}}(\Gamma,A),B)\rightarrow\Sigma_{\mathrm{wrw}}(\Gamma,\Sigma_{\mathrm{wrw}}(A,B))
be the evident w.r.w. dereliction up to ‘tags,’ or
\mathrm{Pair}_{A,B}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\langle\mathrm{fst}\bullet\mathrm{fst},\langle\mathrm{snd}\bullet\mathrm{fst},\mathrm{snd}\rangle\rangle
.
Note that there is the evident inverse
\mathrm{Pair}^{-1}_{A,B}=\langle\langle\mathrm{fst},\mathrm{fst}\bullet\mathrm{snd}\rangle,\mathrm{snd}\bullet\mathrm{snd}\rangle
.

•

(
\Sigma
-Elim)
We define
\mathcal{R}^{\Sigma}_{A,B,P}(\uplus\rho)\in\mathcal{TS}_{\mathrm{wr}}(\Pi_{\mathrm{wrw}}(\Sigma_{\mathrm{wrw}}(\Gamma,\Sigma_{\mathrm{wrw}}(A,B)),P))
to be the composition
\uplus\rho\bullet\mathrm{Pair}_{A,B}^{-1}
for any
P\in\mathscr{D}\mathbb{NPG}_{\mathrm{wrw}}^{\mathrm{wo}}(\Sigma_{\mathrm{wrw}}(\Gamma,\Sigma_{\mathrm{wrw}}(A,B)))
and
\uplus\rho\in\mathcal{TS}_{\mathrm{wr}}(\Pi_{\mathrm{wrw}}(\Sigma_{\mathrm{wrw}}(\Sigma_{\mathrm{wrw}}(\Gamma,A),B),P\{\mathrm{Pair}_{A,B}\}))
.

•

(
\Sigma
-Comp)
We calculate

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}^{\Sigma}_{A,B,P}(\uplus\rho)\{\mathrm{Pair}_{A,B}\}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{R}^{\Sigma}_{A,B,P}(\uplus\rho)\bullet\mathrm{Pair}_{A,B}

\displaystyle=(\uplus\rho\bullet\mathrm{Pair}_{A,B}^{-1})\bullet\mathrm{Pair}_{A,B}

\displaystyle=\uplus\rho\bullet(\mathrm{Pair}_{A,B}^{-1}\bullet\mathrm{Pair}_{A,B})

\displaystyle=\uplus\rho\bullet\uplus\mathrm{der}_{\Sigma_{\mathrm{wrw}}(\Sigma_{\mathrm{wrw}}(\Gamma,A),B)}

\displaystyle=\uplus\rho.

•

(
\Sigma
-Subst)
Similar to the case of pi.

•

(Pair-Subst)
We calculate

\displaystyle\mathrm{p}(\Sigma_{\mathrm{wrw}}(A,B))\bullet\mathrm{Pair}_{A,B}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{fst}\bullet\langle\mathrm{fst}\bullet\mathrm{fst},\langle\mathrm{snd}\bullet\mathrm{fst},\mathrm{snd}\rangle\rangle

\displaystyle=\mathrm{fst}\bullet\mathrm{fst}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{p}(A)\bullet\mathrm{p}(B),

and

\displaystyle{\uplus\phi}^{\star}\bullet\mathrm{Pair}_{A\{\uplus\phi\},B\{\uplus\phi^{+}\}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \langle\uplus\phi\bullet\mathrm{p}(\Sigma_{\mathrm{wrw}}(A,B)\{\uplus\phi\}),\mathrm{v}_{\Sigma_{\mathrm{wrw}}(A,B)\{\uplus\phi\}}\rangle\bullet\mathrm{Pair}_{A\{\uplus\phi\},B\{\uplus\phi^{+}\}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \langle\uplus\phi\bullet\mathrm{p}(\Sigma_{\mathrm{wrw}}(A\{\uplus\phi\},B\{\uplus\phi^{+}\}))\bullet\mathrm{Pair}_{A\{\uplus\phi\},B\{\uplus\phi^{+}\}},\mathrm{v}_{\Sigma_{\mathrm{wrw}}(A,B)\{\uplus\phi\}}\bullet\mathrm{Pair}_{A\{\uplus\phi\},B\{\uplus\phi^{+}\}}\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \langle\uplus\phi\bullet\mathrm{p}(A\{\uplus\phi\})\bullet\mathrm{p}(B\{\uplus\phi^{+}\}),\mathrm{v}_{\Sigma_{\mathrm{wrw}}(A\{\uplus\phi\},B\{\uplus\phi^{+}\})}\bullet\mathrm{Pair}_{A\{\uplus\phi\},B\{\uplus\phi^{+}\}}\rangle

(by the above equations)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \langle\uplus\phi\bullet\mathrm{fst}\bullet\mathrm{fst},\mathrm{snd}\bullet\langle\mathrm{fst}\bullet\mathrm{fst},\langle\mathrm{snd}\bullet\mathrm{fst},\mathrm{snd}\rangle\rangle\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \langle\uplus\phi\bullet\mathrm{fst}\bullet\mathrm{fst},\langle\mathrm{snd}\bullet\mathrm{fst},\mathrm{snd}\rangle\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \langle\mathrm{fst}\bullet\mathrm{fst},\langle\mathrm{snd}\bullet\mathrm{fst},\mathrm{snd}\rangle\rangle\bullet\langle\langle\uplus\phi\bullet\mathrm{fst}\bullet\mathrm{fst},\mathrm{snd}\bullet\mathrm{fst}\rangle,\mathrm{snd}\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \langle\mathrm{fst}\bullet\mathrm{fst},\langle\mathrm{snd}\bullet\mathrm{fst},\mathrm{snd}\rangle\rangle\bullet\langle\langle\uplus\phi\bullet\mathrm{fst},\mathrm{snd}\rangle\bullet\mathrm{fst},\mathrm{snd}\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \mathrm{Pair}_{A,B}\bullet\langle\langle\uplus\phi\bullet\mathrm{p}(A\{\uplus\phi\}),\mathrm{v}_{A\{\uplus\phi\}}\rangle\bullet\mathrm{p}(B\{\uplus\phi^{+}\}),\mathrm{v}_{B\{\uplus\phi^{+}\}}\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \mathrm{Pair}_{A,B}\bullet\langle\uplus\phi^{+}\bullet\mathrm{p}(B\{\uplus\phi^{+}\}),\mathrm{v}_{B\{\uplus\phi^{+}\}}\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ \mathrm{Pair}_{A,B}\bullet\uplus\phi^{++}.

•

(
\mathcal{R}^{\Sigma}
-Subst)
We calculate

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}^{\Sigma}_{A,B,P}(\uplus\rho)\{\uplus\phi^{\star}\}

\displaystyle=\uplus\rho\bullet\mathrm{Pair}_{A,B}^{-1}\bullet\langle\uplus\phi\bullet\mathrm{p}(\Sigma_{\mathrm{wrw}}(A,B)\{\uplus\phi\},\mathrm{v}_{\Sigma_{\mathrm{wrw}}(A,B)\{\uplus\phi\}}\rangle

\displaystyle=\uplus\rho\bullet\langle\langle\mathrm{fst},\mathrm{fst}\bullet\mathrm{snd}\rangle,\mathrm{snd}\bullet\mathrm{snd}\rangle\bullet\langle\uplus\phi\bullet\mathrm{fst},\mathrm{snd}\rangle

\displaystyle=\uplus\rho\bullet\langle\langle\uplus\phi\bullet\mathrm{fst},\mathrm{fst}\bullet\mathrm{snd}\rangle,\mathrm{snd}\bullet\mathrm{snd}\rangle

\displaystyle=\uplus\rho\bullet\langle\langle\uplus\phi\bullet\mathrm{fst},\mathrm{snd}\rangle\bullet\mathrm{fst},\mathrm{snd}\rangle\bullet\langle\langle\mathrm{fst},\mathrm{fst}\bullet\mathrm{snd}\rangle,\mathrm{snd}\bullet\mathrm{snd}\rangle

\displaystyle=\uplus\rho\bullet\langle\uplus\phi^{+}\bullet\mathrm{fst},\mathrm{snd}\rangle\bullet\langle\langle\mathrm{fst},\mathrm{fst}\bullet\mathrm{snd}\rangle,\mathrm{snd}\bullet\mathrm{snd}\rangle

\displaystyle=\uplus\rho\bullet\langle\uplus\phi^{+}\bullet\mathrm{p}(B\{\uplus\phi^{+}\}),\mathrm{v}_{B\{\uplus\phi^{+}\}}\rangle\bullet\mathrm{Pair}_{A\{\uplus\phi\},B\{\uplus\phi^{+}\}}^{-1}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{R}^{\Sigma}_{A\{\uplus\phi\},B\{\uplus\phi^{+}\},P\{\uplus\phi^{\star}\}}(\uplus\rho\bullet\langle\uplus\phi^{+}\bullet\mathrm{p}(B\{\uplus\phi^{+}\}),\mathrm{v}_{B\{\uplus\phi^{+}\}}\rangle)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{R}^{\Sigma}_{A\{\uplus\phi\},B\{\uplus\phi^{+}\},P\{\uplus\phi^{\star}\}}(\uplus\rho\{\uplus\phi^{++}\}).

•

(
\mathcal{R}^{\Sigma}
-Uniq)
If a given t-skeleton
\uplus\varrho\in\mathcal{TS}_{\mathrm{wr}}(\Pi_{\mathrm{wrw}}(\Sigma_{\mathrm{wrw}}(\Gamma,\Sigma_{\mathrm{wrw}}(A,B)),P))
satisfies the equation
\uplus\varrho\{\mathrm{Pair}_{A,B}\}=\uplus\rho
then we have

\uplus\varrho=\uplus\rho\bullet\mathrm{Pair}_{A,B}^{-1}=\mathcal{R}^{\Sigma}_{A,B,P}(\uplus\rho),

which completes the proof.
∎

We proceed to model N-type (Appendix
A.5
).
Again, let us first recall the semantic type former for N-type in an arbitrary CwF:

Definition 3.45

(CwFs with N-type
[
20
]
12
12

12

The definition is left to the reader in
[
20
]
, and thus Definition
3.45
is the present author’s solution, which may be shown to be
sound
in the same manner as the case of pi- and sigma-types
[
20
]
. This point is applied to empty-type given below as well.
)
.

A CwF
\mathcal{C}

supports N
or
natural numbers
if

•

(
N
-Form)
Given
\Gamma\in\mathcal{C}
, there is a type
N^{\Gamma}\in\mathrm{Ty}(\Gamma)
, called
natural number (N-) type
in
\Gamma
, which we often abbreviate as
N
;

•

(
N
-Intro)
There are a term and a morphism in
\mathcal{C}

\underline{0}_{\Gamma}\in\mathrm{Tm}(\Gamma,N)\and\mathrm{succ}_{\Gamma}:\Gamma.N\to\Gamma.N

that satisfy the equations

\underline{0}_{\Gamma}\{f\}=\underline{0}_{\Delta}\in\mathrm{Tm}(\Delta,N)\and\mathrm{p}(N)\circ\mathrm{succ}_{\Gamma}=\mathrm{p}(N):\Gamma.N\to\Gamma\and\mathrm{succ}_{\Gamma}\circ\langle g,\mathrm{v}_{N}\rangle_{N}=\langle g,\mathrm{v}_{N}\{\mathrm{succ}_{\Delta}\}\rangle_{N}:\Delta.N\to\Gamma.N

for any morphisms
f:\Delta\to\Gamma
and
g:\Delta.N\to\Gamma
in
\mathcal{C}
;

Notation
.

Let us define
\mathrm{zero}_{\Gamma}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\langle\mathrm{id}_{\Gamma},\underline{0}_{\Gamma}\rangle_{N}:\Gamma\to\Gamma.N
for each
\Gamma\in\mathcal{C}
; it then satisfies
\mathrm{zero}_{\Gamma}\circ f=\langle f,\underline{0}_{\Delta}\rangle_{N}=\langle f,\mathrm{v}_{N}\{\mathrm{zero}_{\Delta}\}\rangle_{N}:\Delta\to\Gamma.N
for any morphism
f:\Delta\to\Gamma
in
\mathcal{C}
.
We often omit the subscript
(\_)_{\Gamma}
on
\underline{0}_{\Gamma}
,
\mathrm{zero}_{\Gamma}
and
\mathrm{succ}_{\Gamma}
.
Also, for each
n\in\mathbb{N}
, we define
\underline{n}_{\Gamma}\in\mathrm{Tm}(\Gamma,N)
by

–

\underline{0}_{\Gamma}
is already given;

–

\underline{n+1}_{\Gamma}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\mathrm{v}_{N}\{\mathrm{succ}_{\Gamma}\circ\langle\mathrm{id}_{\Gamma},\underline{n}_{\Gamma}\rangle\}
;

•

(
N
-Elim)
Given a type
P\in\mathrm{Ty}(\Gamma.N)
, and terms
z\in\mathrm{Tm}(\Gamma,P\{\mathrm{zero}\})
and
s\in\mathrm{Tm}(\Gamma.N.P,P\{\mathrm{succ}\circ\mathrm{p}(P)\})
, there is a term
\mathcal{R}^{N}_{P}(z,s)\in\mathrm{Tm}(\Gamma.N,P)
;

•

(
N
-Comp)
We have the equations

\mathcal{R}^{N}_{P}(z,s)\{\mathrm{zero}\}=z\in\mathrm{Tm}(\Gamma,P\{\mathrm{zero}\});\and\mathcal{R}^{N}_{P}(z,s)\{\mathrm{succ}\}=s\{\langle\mathrm{id}_{\Gamma.N},\mathcal{R}^{N}_{P}(z,s)\rangle_{P}\}\in\mathrm{Tm}(\Gamma.N,P\{\mathrm{succ}\});

•

(
N
-Subst)

N^{\Gamma}\{f\}=N^{\Delta}\in\mathrm{Ty}(\Delta)
;

•

(
\mathcal{R}^{N}
-Subst)

\mathcal{R}^{N}_{P}(z,s)\{f^{+}\}=\mathcal{R}^{N}_{P\{f^{+}\}}(z\{f\},s\{f^{++}\})\in\mathrm{Tm}(\Delta.N,P\{f^{+}\})
, where

f^{+}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\langle f\circ\mathrm{p}(N),\mathrm{v}_{N}\rangle_{N}:\Delta.N\to\Gamma.N\and f^{++}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\langle f^{+}\circ\mathrm{p}(P\{f^{+}\}),\mathrm{v}_{P\{f^{+}\}}\rangle_{P}:\Delta.N.P\{f^{+}\}\to\Gamma.N.P.

N-type is modeled in a CwF that supports natural numbers; see Appendix
A.5
for the details.

We now propose our game semantics of N-type by basically employing the
total
fragment of the standard game semantics of PCF à la McCusker
[
33
]
:

Theorem 3.46

(Realizability model of N-type à la game semantics)
.

The CwF
\mathbb{NPG}_{\mathrm{wrw}}^{\mathrm{wo}}
supports natural numbers.

Proof.

Let
\Gamma,\Delta\in\mathbb{NPG}_{\mathrm{wrw}}^{\mathrm{wo}}
and
\uplus\phi\in\mathbb{NPG}_{\mathrm{wrw}}^{\mathrm{wo}}(\Delta,\Gamma)
, and assume that
\mathscr{R}_{\mathrm{wr}}^{\mathrm{cp}}(\Gamma)\neq\emptyset
and
\mathscr{R}_{\mathrm{wr}}^{\mathrm{cp}}(\Delta)\neq\emptyset
since the other cases are trivial.

•

(
N
-Form)
Let
N^{\Gamma}
be the constant dependent np-game on
\Gamma
valued at
N
(Example
3.9
), for which we write
\{N\}_{\Gamma}
or
\{N\}
.

•

(
N
-Intro)
Let
\underline{0}_{\Gamma}\in\mathcal{TS}_{\mathrm{wr}}(\Pi_{\mathrm{wrw}}(\Gamma,\{N\}))
be
\uplus\{\underline{0}\}::\hat{\oc}\Gamma\rightarrowtriangle N
up to ‘tags,’ and
\mathrm{succ}_{\Gamma}:\Sigma_{\mathrm{wrw}}(\Gamma,\{N^{[0]}\})\rightarrow\Sigma_{\mathrm{wrw}}(\Gamma,\{N^{[1]}\})
the pairing
\langle\mathrm{p}(\{N\}),\uplus\mathrm{s}^{\Gamma}\rangle
, where the superscripts
(\_)^{[i]}
(
i=0,1
) are to distinguish the two copies of
N
, and the DoWRWLI
\uplus\mathrm{s}^{\Gamma}::\Pi_{\mathrm{wrw}}(\Sigma_{\mathrm{wrw}}(\Gamma,\{N^{[0]}\}),\{N^{[1]}\})
is defined by

\mathrm{s}^{\Gamma}_{(\sigma^{\dagger},e)}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\mathrm{Pref}(\{q^{[1]}.q^{[0]}.n^{[0]}.(n+1)^{[1]}\})\quad(\underline{n}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\mathrm{snd}\bullet\sigma)

up to ‘tags’ for each
(\sigma^{\dagger},e)\in\mathscr{R}_{\mathrm{wr}}^{\mathrm{cp}}(\hat{\oc}\Sigma_{\mathrm{wrw}}(\Gamma,\{N^{[0]}\}))
.
Clearly,
\underline{0}_{\Gamma}\bullet\uplus\phi=\underline{0}_{\Delta}
and
\uplus\mathrm{s}^{\Gamma}\bullet\langle\uplus\psi,\mathrm{v}_{\{N\}_{\Delta}}\rangle=\uplus\mathrm{s}^{\Delta}=\mathrm{v}_{\{N\}_{\Delta}}\{\mathrm{succ}_{\Delta}\}
, where
\uplus\psi:\Sigma_{\mathrm{wrw}}(\Delta,\{N\}_{\Delta})\rightarrow\Gamma
is any morphism in
\mathbb{NPG}_{\mathrm{wrw}}^{\mathrm{wo}}
, and therefore the required equations hold.

•

(
N
-Elim)
Given
P\in\mathscr{D}\mathbb{NPG}_{\mathrm{wrw}}^{\mathrm{wo}}(\Sigma_{\mathrm{wrw}}(\Gamma,\{N\}))
,
\uplus\zeta\in\mathcal{TS}_{\mathrm{wr}}(\Pi_{\mathrm{wrw}}(\Gamma,P\{\mathrm{zero}\}))
and
\uplus\sigma\in\mathcal{TS}_{\mathrm{wr}}(\Pi_{\mathrm{wrw}}(\Sigma_{\mathrm{wrw}}(\Sigma_{\mathrm{wrw}}(\Gamma,\{N\}),P),P\{\mathrm{succ}\circ\mathrm{p}(P)\}))
, there are

\uplus\tilde{\zeta}\in\mathcal{TS}_{\mathrm{wr}}(\Pi_{\mathrm{wrw}}(\Sigma_{\mathrm{wrw}}(\Pi_{\mathrm{wrw}}(\Sigma_{\mathrm{wrw}}(\Gamma,\{N\}),P),\{\Sigma_{\mathrm{wrw}}(\Gamma,\{N\})\}),P\{\mathrm{zero}\bullet\mathrm{fst}\bullet\mathrm{snd}\}))\\
\uplus\tilde{\sigma}\in\mathcal{TS}_{\mathrm{wr}}(\Pi_{\mathrm{wrw}}(\Sigma_{\mathrm{wrw}}(\Pi_{\mathrm{wrw}}(\Sigma_{\mathrm{wrw}}(\Gamma,\{N\}),P),\{\Sigma_{\mathrm{wrw}}(\Gamma,\{N\})\}),P\{\mathrm{succ}\bullet\mathrm{pred}\bullet\mathrm{snd}\}))

defined respectively by

\uplus\tilde{\zeta}:\Pi_{\mathrm{wrw}}(\Sigma_{\mathrm{wrw}}(\Gamma,\{N\}),P)\mathbin{\&}\Sigma_{\mathrm{wrw}}(\Gamma,\{N\})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{snd}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\Sigma_{\mathrm{wrw}}(\Gamma,\{N\})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{fst}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\Gamma\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\uplus\zeta}}{{\longrightarrow}}\textstyle\int P\{\mathrm{zero}\};\\
\uplus\tilde{\sigma}:\Pi_{\mathrm{wrw}}(\Sigma_{\mathrm{wrw}}(\Gamma,\{N\}),P)\mathbin{\&}\Sigma_{\mathrm{wrw}}(\Gamma,\{N\})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\langle\mathrm{pred}\bullet\mathrm{snd},\mathrm{ev}_{P}\{\langle\mathrm{fst},\mathrm{pred}\bullet\mathrm{snd}\rangle\}\rangle}}{{\longrightarrow}}\Sigma_{\mathrm{wrw}}(\Gamma,\{N\})\mathbin{\&}\textstyle\int P\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\uplus\sigma}}{{\longrightarrow}}\textstyle\int P\{\mathrm{succ}\circ\mathrm{fst}\},

where
\mathrm{ev}_{P}\in\mathcal{TS}_{\mathrm{wr}}(\Pi_{\mathrm{wrw}}(\Sigma_{\mathrm{wrw}}(\Pi_{\mathrm{wrw}}(\Sigma_{\mathrm{wrw}}(\Gamma,\{N\}),P),\{\Sigma_{\mathrm{wrw}}(\Gamma,\{N\})\}),P\{\mathrm{snd}\}))
is the
evaluation
on
P

[
33
,
36
]
given by
\mathrm{ev}_{P}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\lambda^{-1}(\uplus\mathrm{der}_{\Pi_{\mathrm{wrw}}(\Sigma_{\mathrm{wrw}}(\Gamma,\{N\}),P)})
, and
\mathrm{pred}:\Sigma_{\mathrm{wrw}}(\Gamma,\{N\})\to\Sigma_{\mathrm{wrw}}(\Gamma,\{N\})
is the
predecessor

[
33
]
given similarly to
\mathrm{succ}
such that
\mathrm{pred}\bullet\mathrm{succ}=\uplus\mathrm{der}_{\Sigma_{\mathrm{wrw}}(\Gamma,\{N\})}
and
\mathrm{pred}\bullet\mathrm{zero}=\mathrm{zero}
.

In addition, let us define

P_{\mathrm{z}}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}P\{\mathrm{zero}\bullet\mathrm{p}(N)\})\in\mathscr{D}\mathbb{NPG}_{\mathrm{wrw}}^{\mathrm{wo}}(\Sigma_{\mathrm{wrw}}(\Gamma,\{N\}));\\
P_{\mathrm{s}}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}P\{\mathrm{succ}\bullet\mathrm{pred}\bullet\mathrm{p}(P_{\mathrm{z}})\}\in\mathscr{D}\mathbb{NPG}_{\mathrm{wrw}}^{\mathrm{wo}}(\Sigma_{\mathrm{wrw}}(\Sigma_{\mathrm{wrw}}(\Gamma,\{N\}),P_{\mathrm{z}})),

and then we have

\mathrm{cond}_{P}\in\mathcal{TS}_{\mathrm{wr}}(\Pi_{\mathrm{wrw}}(\Sigma_{\mathrm{wrw}}(\Sigma_{\mathrm{wrw}}(\Sigma_{\mathrm{wrw}}(\Gamma,\{N\}),P_{\mathrm{z}}),P_{\mathrm{s}}),P\{\mathrm{p}(P_{\mathrm{z}})\bullet\mathrm{p}(P_{\mathrm{s}})\}))

that is the standard interpretation of
conditionals
on
P

[
33
,
36
]
: It first asks an input natural number in the component
N
of the domain, and plays as the w.r.w. dereliction between
P_{\mathrm{z}}
and
P\{\mathrm{p}(P_{\mathrm{z}})\bullet\mathrm{p}(P_{\mathrm{s}})\}
if the answer is
\underline{0}
, and as the w.r.w. dereliction between
P_{\mathrm{s}}
and
P\{\mathrm{p}(P_{\mathrm{z}})\bullet\mathrm{p}(P_{\mathrm{s}})\}
otherwise.
Then, we define
\mathcal{F}^{N}_{P}(\uplus\zeta,\uplus\sigma):\Pi_{\mathrm{wrw}}(\Sigma_{\mathrm{wrw}}(\Gamma,\{N\}),P)\to\Pi_{\mathrm{wrw}}(\Sigma_{\mathrm{wrw}}(\Gamma,\{N\}),P)
by

\mathcal{F}^{N}_{P}(\uplus\zeta,\uplus\sigma)\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\lambda_{\{\Sigma_{\mathrm{wrw}}(\Gamma,\{N\})\},\{P\{\mathrm{snd}\}\}}(\mathrm{cond}_{P}\{\langle\langle\mathrm{snd},\uplus\tilde{\zeta}\rangle,\uplus\tilde{\sigma}\rangle\}).

Finally, we define
\mathcal{R}^{N}_{P}(\uplus\zeta,\uplus\sigma)\in\mathcal{TS}_{\mathrm{wr}}(\Pi_{\mathrm{wrw}}(\Sigma_{\mathrm{wrw}}(\Gamma,\{N\}),P))
to be the least upper bound of the chain
\big(\mathcal{R}^{N}_{P}(\uplus\zeta,\uplus\sigma)_{n}\in\mathcal{TS}_{\mathrm{wr}}(\Pi_{\mathrm{wrw}}(\Sigma_{\mathrm{wrw}}(\Gamma,\{N\}),P))\big)_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
:

\mathcal{R}^{N}_{P}(\uplus\zeta,\uplus\sigma)_{0}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\top\quad\text{(up to `tags')};\\
\mathcal{R}^{N}_{P}(\uplus\zeta,\uplus\sigma)_{n+1}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\mathcal{F}^{N}_{P}(\uplus\zeta,\uplus\sigma)\bullet\mathcal{R}^{N}_{P}(\uplus\zeta,\uplus\sigma)_{n}.

•

(
N
-Comp)
By the definition of
\mathcal{R}^{N}_{P}(\uplus\zeta,\uplus\sigma)
, we calculate

\mathcal{R}^{N}_{P}(\uplus\zeta,\uplus\sigma)\{\mathrm{zero}\}=\uplus\zeta;\\
\mathcal{R}^{N}_{P}(\uplus\zeta,\uplus\sigma)\{\mathrm{succ}\}=\uplus\sigma\{\langle\uplus\mathrm{der}_{\Sigma(\Gamma,\{N\})},\mathcal{R}^{N}_{P}(\uplus\zeta,\uplus\sigma)\rangle\}.

•

(
N
-Subst)

\{N\}_{\Gamma}\{\uplus\phi\}=\{N\}_{\Delta}
.

•

(
\mathcal{R}^{N}
-Subst)

Finally, by the definition of
\mathcal{R}^{N}_{P}(\uplus\zeta,\uplus\sigma)
, we calculate

\mathcal{R}^{N}_{P}(\uplus\zeta,\uplus\sigma)\{\uplus\phi^{+}\}=\mathcal{R}^{N}_{P\{\uplus\phi^{+}\}}(\uplus\zeta\{\uplus\phi\},\uplus\sigma\{\uplus\phi^{++}\})

(or
\mathcal{R}^{N}_{P}(\uplus\zeta,\uplus\sigma)_{n}\{\uplus\phi^{+}\}=\mathcal{R}^{N}_{P\{\uplus\phi^{+}\}}(\uplus\zeta\{\uplus\phi\},\uplus\sigma\{\uplus\phi^{++}\})_{n}
for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
).
∎

We proceed to model
identity (Id-) types
(Appendix
A.8
).
Again, we first review the semantic type former for Id-types in an arbitrary CwF:

Definition 3.47

(CwFs with Id-types
[
20
]
)
.

A CwF
\mathcal{C}

supports Id
if

•

(Id-Form)
Given
\Gamma\in\mathcal{C}
and
A\in\mathrm{Ty}(\Gamma)
, there is a type
\mathrm{Id}_{A}\in\mathrm{Ty}(\Gamma.A.A^{+})
, where
A^{+}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}A\{\mathrm{p}(A)\}\in\mathrm{Ty}(\Gamma.A)
;

•

(Id-Intro)
There is a morphism
\mathrm{Refl}_{A}:\Gamma.A\to\Gamma.A.A^{+}.\mathrm{Id}_{A}
in
\mathcal{C}
;

•

(Id-Elim)
Given
B\in\mathrm{Ty}(\Gamma.A.A^{+}.\mathrm{Id}_{A})
and
b\in\mathrm{Tm}(\Gamma.A,B\{\mathrm{Refl}_{A}\})
, there is a term
\mathcal{R}^{\mathrm{Id}}_{A,B}(b)\in\mathrm{Tm}(\Gamma.A.A^{+}.\mathrm{Id}_{A},B)
;

•

(Id-Comp)

\mathcal{R}^{\mathrm{Id}}_{A,B}(b)\{\mathrm{Refl}_{A}\}=b
;

•

(Id-Subst)

\mathrm{Id}_{A}\{f^{++}\}=\mathrm{Id}_{A\{f\}}\in\mathrm{Ty}(\Delta.A\{f\}.A\{f\}^{+})
for all
\Delta\in\mathcal{C}
and
f:\Delta\to\Gamma
in
\mathcal{C}
, where

A\{f\}^{+}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}A\{f\}\{\mathrm{p}(A\{f\})\}\in\mathrm{Ty}(\Delta.A\{f\});\and f^{+}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\langle f\circ\mathrm{p}(A\{f\}),\mathrm{v}_{A\{f\}}\rangle_{A}:\Delta.A\{f\}\to\Gamma.A;\and f^{++}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\langle f^{+}\circ\mathrm{p}(A^{+}\{f^{+}\}),\mathrm{v}_{A^{+}\{f^{+}\}}\rangle_{A^{+}}:\Delta.A\{f\}.A^{+}\{f^{+}\}\to\Gamma.A.A^{+};

•

(Refl-Subst)

\mathrm{Refl}_{A}\circ f^{+}=f^{+++}\circ\mathrm{Refl}_{A\{f\}}:\Delta.A\{f\}\to\Gamma.A.A^{+}.\mathrm{Id}_{A}
, where

f^{+++}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\langle f^{++}\circ\mathrm{p}(\mathrm{Id}_{A}\{f^{++}\}),\mathrm{v}_{\mathrm{Id}_{A}\{f^{++}\}}\rangle_{\mathrm{Id}_{A}}:\Delta.A\{f\}.A^{+}\{f^{+}\}.\mathrm{Id}_{A\{f\}}\to\Gamma.A.A^{+}.\mathrm{Id}_{A};

•

(
\mathcal{R}^{\mathrm{Id}}
-Subst)

\mathcal{R}^{\mathrm{Id}}_{A,B}(b)\{f^{+++}\}=\mathcal{R}^{\mathrm{Id}}_{A\{f\},B\{f^{+++}\}}(b\{f^{+}\})
.

Id-types are modeled in a CwF that supports Id; see Appendix
A.8
for the details.

We then equip the CwF
\mathbb{NPG}_{\mathrm{wrw}}^{\mathrm{wo}}
with our game-semantic Id-types:

Theorem 3.48

(Realizability model of Id-types à la game semantics)
.

The CwF
\mathbb{NPG}_{\mathrm{wrw}}^{\mathrm{wo}}
supports Id.

Proof.

Let
\Gamma,\Delta\in\mathbb{NPG}_{\mathrm{wrw}}^{\mathrm{wo}}
,
\uplus\phi\in\mathbb{NPG}_{\mathrm{wrw}}^{\mathrm{wo}}(\Delta,\Gamma)
and
A\in\mathscr{D}\mathbb{NPG}_{\mathrm{wrw}}^{\mathrm{wo}}(\Gamma)
.

•

(Id-Form)
We define
\mathrm{Id}_{A}\in\mathscr{D}\mathbb{NPG}_{\mathrm{wrw}}^{\mathrm{wo}}(\Sigma_{\mathrm{wrw}}(\Sigma_{\mathrm{wrw}}(\Gamma,A),A^{+}))
by
\mathrm{Id}_{A}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}(\mathrm{Id}_{A(\uplus\gamma)}(\uplus\alpha,\uplus\alpha^{\prime}))_{\langle\langle\uplus\gamma,\uplus\alpha\rangle,\uplus\alpha^{\prime}\rangle\in\mathbb{NPG}_{\mathrm{wrw}}^{\mathrm{wo}}(\Sigma_{\mathrm{wrw}}(\Sigma_{\mathrm{wrw}}(\Gamma,A),A^{+}))}
.

•
