(Id-Intro)
Let
\mathrm{Refl}_{A}:\Sigma_{\mathrm{wrw}}(\Gamma,A^{[1]})\rightarrow\Sigma_{\mathrm{wrw}}(\Sigma_{\mathrm{wrw}}(\Sigma_{\mathrm{wrw}}(\Gamma,A^{[2]}),(A^{[3]})^{+}),\mathrm{Id}_{A})
be what plays by the w.r.w. dereliction between
\Sigma_{\mathrm{wrw}}(\Gamma,A^{[1]})
and
\Sigma_{\mathrm{wrw}}(\Gamma,A^{[2]})
, between
\int_{\Gamma}A^{[1]}
and
\int_{\Gamma}(A^{[3]})^{+}
, and trivially on
\Sigma_{\mathrm{wrw}}(\Gamma,A^{[1]})\rightarrow\bm{1}
, where the superscripts
(\_)^{[i]}
(
i=1,2,3
) are to distinguish the three copies of
A
.
The inverse
\mathrm{Refl}_{A}^{-1}:\Sigma_{\mathrm{wrw}}(\Sigma_{\mathrm{wrw}}(\Sigma_{\mathrm{wrw}}(\Gamma,A^{[2]}),(A^{[3]})^{+}),\mathrm{Id}_{A})\to\Sigma_{\mathrm{wrw}}(\Gamma,A^{[1]})
is the w.r.w. dereliction between
\Sigma_{\mathrm{wrw}}(\Gamma,A^{[2]})
and
\Sigma_{\mathrm{wrw}}(\Gamma,A^{[1]})
up to ‘tags.’

•

(Id-Elim)
Given
B\in\mathscr{D}\mathbb{NPG}_{\mathrm{wrw}}^{\mathrm{wo}}(\Sigma_{\mathrm{wrw}}(\Sigma_{\mathrm{wrw}}(\Sigma_{\mathrm{wrw}}(\Gamma,A^{[2]}),(A^{[3]})^{+}),\mathrm{Id}_{A}))
and
\uplus\beta\in\mathcal{TS}_{\mathrm{wr}}(\Pi_{\mathrm{wrw}}(\Sigma_{\mathrm{wrw}}(\Gamma,A),B\{\mathrm{Refl}_{A}\}))
, we define

\mathcal{R}^{\mathrm{Id}}_{A,B}(\uplus\beta)\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\uplus\beta\bullet\mathrm{Refl}_{A}^{-1}\in\mathcal{TS}_{\mathrm{wr}}(\Pi_{\mathrm{wrw}}((\Sigma_{\mathrm{wrw}}(\Sigma_{\mathrm{wrw}}(\Sigma_{\mathrm{wrw}}(\Gamma,A),A^{+}),\mathrm{Id}_{A}),B)).

•

(Id-Comp)
We then calculate

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}^{\mathrm{Id}}_{A,B}(\uplus\beta)\{\mathrm{Refl}_{A}\}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{R}^{\mathrm{Id}}_{A,B}(\uplus\beta)\bullet\mathrm{Refl}_{A}

\displaystyle=(\uplus\beta\bullet\mathrm{Refl}_{A}^{-1})\bullet\mathrm{Refl}_{A}

\displaystyle=\uplus\beta\bullet(\mathrm{Refl}_{A}^{-1}\bullet\mathrm{Refl}_{A})

\displaystyle=\uplus\beta\bullet\uplus\mathrm{der}_{\Sigma_{\mathrm{wrw}}(\Gamma,A)}

\displaystyle=\uplus\beta.

•

(Id-Subst)
We calculate

\displaystyle\mathrm{Id}_{A}\{\uplus\phi^{++}\}

\displaystyle=(\mathrm{Id}_{A(\uplus\gamma)}(\uplus\alpha,\uplus\alpha^{\prime}))_{\langle\langle\uplus\gamma,\uplus\alpha\rangle,\uplus\alpha^{\prime}\rangle\in\mathbb{NPG}_{\mathrm{wrw}}^{\mathrm{wo}}(\Sigma_{\mathrm{wrw}}(\Sigma_{\mathrm{wrw}}(\Gamma,A),A^{+}))}\{\uplus\phi^{++}\}

\displaystyle=(\mathrm{Id}_{A}(\langle\langle\uplus\gamma,\uplus\alpha\rangle,\uplus\alpha^{\prime}\rangle))_{\langle\langle\uplus\gamma,\uplus\alpha\rangle,\uplus\alpha^{\prime}\rangle\in\mathbb{NPG}_{\mathrm{wrw}}^{\mathrm{wo}}(\Sigma_{\mathrm{wrw}}(\Sigma_{\mathrm{wrw}}(\Gamma,A),A^{+})}\{\uplus\phi^{++}\}

\displaystyle=(\mathrm{Id}_{A}(\uplus\phi^{++}\bullet\langle\langle\uplus\delta,\uplus\alpha\rangle,\uplus\alpha^{\prime}\rangle))_{\langle\langle\uplus\delta,\uplus\alpha\rangle,\uplus\alpha^{\prime}\rangle\in\mathbb{NPG}_{\mathrm{wrw}}^{\mathrm{wo}}(\Sigma_{\mathrm{wrw}}(\Sigma_{\mathrm{wrw}}(\Delta,A\{\uplus\phi\}),A\{\uplus\phi\}^{+}))}

\displaystyle=(\mathrm{Id}_{A}(\langle\uplus\phi\bullet\uplus\delta,\uplus\alpha\rangle,\uplus\alpha^{\prime}\rangle))_{\langle\langle\uplus\delta,\uplus\alpha\rangle,\uplus\alpha^{\prime}\rangle\in\mathbb{NPG}_{\mathrm{wrw}}^{\mathrm{wo}}(\Sigma_{\mathrm{wrw}}(\Sigma_{\mathrm{wrw}}(\Delta,A\{\uplus\phi\}),A\{\uplus\phi\}^{+}))}

\displaystyle=(\mathrm{Id}_{A(\uplus\phi\bullet\uplus\delta)}(\uplus\alpha,\uplus\alpha^{\prime}))_{\langle\langle\uplus\delta,\uplus\alpha\rangle,\uplus\alpha^{\prime}\rangle\in\mathbb{NPG}_{\mathrm{wrw}}^{\mathrm{wo}}(\Sigma_{\mathrm{wrw}}(\Sigma_{\mathrm{wrw}}(\Delta,A\{\uplus\phi\}),A\{\uplus\phi\}^{+}))}

\displaystyle=(\mathrm{Id}_{A\{\uplus\phi\}(\uplus\delta)}(\uplus\alpha,\uplus\alpha^{\prime}))_{\langle\langle\uplus\delta,\uplus\alpha\rangle,\uplus\alpha^{\prime}\rangle\in\mathbb{NPG}_{\mathrm{wrw}}^{\mathrm{wo}}(\Sigma_{\mathrm{wrw}}(\Sigma_{\mathrm{wrw}}(\Delta,A\{\uplus\phi\}),A\{\uplus\phi\}^{+}))}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{Id}_{A\{\uplus\phi\}},

where the forth equation holds because

\displaystyle\uplus\phi^{++}\bullet\langle\langle\uplus\delta,\uplus\alpha\rangle,\uplus\alpha^{\prime}\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle\uplus\phi^{+}\bullet\mathrm{p}(A^{+}\{\uplus\phi^{+}\}),\mathrm{v}_{A^{+}\{\uplus\phi^{+}\}}\rangle\bullet\langle\langle\uplus\delta,\uplus\alpha\rangle,\uplus\alpha^{\prime}\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle\uplus\phi^{+}\bullet\mathrm{p}(A^{+}\{\uplus\phi^{+}\})\bullet\langle\langle\uplus\delta,\uplus\alpha\rangle,\uplus\alpha^{\prime}\rangle,\mathrm{v}_{A^{+}\{\uplus\phi^{+}\}}\bullet\langle\langle\uplus\delta,\uplus\alpha\rangle,\uplus\alpha^{\prime}\rangle\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle\langle\uplus\phi\bullet\uplus\delta,\uplus\alpha\rangle,\uplus\alpha^{\prime}\rangle.

•

(Refl-Subst)
We calculate

\displaystyle\mathrm{Refl}_{A}\bullet\uplus\phi^{+}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{Refl}_{A}\bullet\langle\uplus\phi\bullet\mathrm{p}(A\{\uplus\phi\}),\mathrm{v}_{A\{\uplus\phi\}}\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle\langle\langle\uplus\phi\bullet\mathrm{p}(A\{\uplus\phi\}),\mathrm{v}_{A\{\uplus\phi\}}\rangle\bullet\mathrm{p}(A^{+}\{\uplus\phi^{+}\})\bullet\mathrm{p}(\mathrm{Id}_{A}\{\uplus\phi^{++}\}),

\displaystyle\mathrm{v}_{A^{+}\{\uplus\phi^{+}\}}\bullet\mathrm{p}(\mathrm{Id}_{A}\{\uplus\phi^{++}\})\rangle,\mathrm{v}_{\mathrm{Id}_{A}\{\uplus\phi^{++}\}}\rangle\bullet\mathrm{Refl}_{A\{\uplus\phi\}}\quad\text{(by the definition of $\mathrm{Refl}$)}

\displaystyle=\langle\langle\uplus\phi^{+}\bullet\mathrm{p}(A^{+}\{\uplus\phi^{+}\}),\mathrm{v}_{A^{+}\{\uplus\phi^{+}\}}\rangle\bullet\mathrm{p}(\mathrm{Id}_{A}\{\uplus\phi^{++}\}),\mathrm{v}_{\mathrm{Id}_{A}\{\uplus\phi^{++}\}}\rangle\bullet\mathrm{Refl}_{A\{\uplus\phi\}}

\displaystyle=\langle\uplus\phi^{++}\bullet\mathrm{p}(\mathrm{Id}_{A}\{\uplus\phi^{++}\}),\mathrm{v}_{\mathrm{Id}_{A}\{\uplus\phi^{++}\}}\rangle\bullet\mathrm{Refl}_{A\{\uplus\phi\}}

\displaystyle=\uplus\phi^{+++}\bullet\mathrm{Refl}_{A\{\uplus\phi\}}.

•

(
\mathcal{R}^{\mathrm{Id}}
-Subst)
We calculate

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}^{\mathrm{Id}}_{A,B}(\uplus\beta)\{\uplus\phi^{+++}\}

\displaystyle=(\uplus\beta\bullet\mathrm{Refl}_{A}^{-1})\bullet\uplus\phi^{+++}

\displaystyle=\uplus\beta\bullet(\mathrm{Refl}_{A}^{-1}\bullet\uplus\phi^{+++})

\displaystyle=\uplus\beta\bullet(\uplus\phi^{+}\bullet\mathrm{Refl}_{A\{\uplus\phi\}}^{-1})\quad\text{(by Refl-Subst)}

\displaystyle=(\uplus\beta\bullet\uplus\phi^{+})\bullet\mathrm{Refl}_{A\{\uplus\phi\}}^{-1}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{R}^{\mathrm{Id}}_{A\{\uplus\phi\},B\{\uplus\phi^{+++}\}}(\uplus\beta\bullet\uplus\phi^{+})

\displaystyle=\mathcal{R}^{\mathrm{Id}}_{A\{\uplus\phi\},B\{\uplus\phi^{+++}\}}(\uplus\beta\{\uplus\phi^{+}\}),

which completes the proof.
∎

We next model
unit-type
(Appendix
A.3
).
Again, let us first recall the semantic type former for unit-type:

Definition 3.49

(CwFs with unit-type
[
20
]
)
.

A CwF
\mathcal{C}

supports unit
if

•

(Unit-Form)
Given
\Gamma\in\mathcal{C}
, there is a type
\bm{1}^{\Gamma}\in\mathrm{Ty}(\Gamma)
, called the
unity type
in the context
\Gamma
;

•

(Unit-Intro)
Given
\Gamma\in\mathcal{C}
, there is a term
\top_{\Gamma}\in\mathrm{Tm}(\Gamma,\bm{1}^{\Gamma})
;

•

(Unit-Elim)
Given
\Gamma\in\mathcal{C}
,
A\in\mathrm{Ty}(\Gamma.\bm{1}^{\Gamma})
,
a\in\mathrm{Tm}(\Gamma,A\{\overline{\top_{\Gamma}}\})
and
t\in\mathrm{Tm}(\Gamma,\bm{1}^{\Gamma})
, there is a term
\mathcal{R}^{\bm{1}}_{A}(a,t)\in\mathrm{Tm}(\Gamma,A\{\overline{t}\})
, where

\overline{\top}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\langle\mathrm{id}_{\Gamma},\top_{\Gamma}\rangle_{\bm{1}^{\Gamma}}:\Gamma\to\Gamma.\bm{1}^{\Gamma}\and\overline{t}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\langle\mathrm{id}_{\Gamma},t\rangle_{\bm{1}^{\Gamma}}:\Gamma\to\Gamma.\bm{1}^{\Gamma};

•

(Unit-Comp)

\mathcal{R}^{\bm{1}}_{A}(a,\top_{\Gamma})=a
;

•

(Unit-Subst)
Given
f:\Delta\to\Gamma
in
\mathcal{C}
,
\bm{1}^{\Gamma}\{f\}=\bm{1}^{\Delta}\in\mathrm{Ty}(\Delta)
;

•

(
\top
-Subst)

\top_{\Gamma}\{f\}=\top_{\Delta}\in\mathrm{Tm}(\Delta,\bm{1}^{\Delta})
.

Moreover,
\mathcal{C}

supports one in the strict sense
if it additionally satisfies:

•

(
\top
-Uniq)

t=\top_{\Gamma}
for all
t\in\mathrm{Tm}(\Gamma,\bm{1}^{\Gamma})
.
13
13

13

Note that
\top
-Uniq implies Unit-Elim and Unit-Comp by defining
\mathcal{R}^{\bm{1}}_{A}(a,t)\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}a
.

Unit-type (with the
\eta
-rule) is modeled in a CwF that supports unit (in the strict sense); see Appendix
A.3
for the details.

We now propose our game semantics of unit-type:

Theorem 3.50

(Realizability model of unit-type à la game semantics)
.

The CwF
\mathbb{NPG}_{\mathrm{wrw}}^{\mathrm{wo}}
supports unit in the strict sense.

Proof.

Let
\Gamma,\Delta\in\mathbb{NPG}_{\mathrm{wrw}}^{\mathrm{wo}}
and
\uplus\phi\in\mathbb{NPG}_{\mathrm{wrw}}^{\mathrm{wo}}(\Delta,\Gamma)
, and assume that
\mathscr{R}_{\mathrm{wr}}^{\mathrm{cp}}(\Gamma)\neq\emptyset
and
\mathscr{R}_{\mathrm{wr}}^{\mathrm{cp}}(\Delta)\neq\emptyset
since the other cases are trivial.

•

(Unit-Form)
Let
\bm{1}^{\Gamma}
be the constant dependent np-game valued at the unit np-game
\bm{1}
(Example
3.9
), for which we write
\{\bm{1}\}_{\Gamma}
or
\{\bm{1}\}
.

•

(Unit-Intro)
Let
\top_{\Gamma}\in\mathrm{Tm}(\Gamma,\{\bm{1}\}_{\Gamma})
be the unique one
\uplus\{\top\}
up to ‘tags.’

•

(Unit-Elim)
Given
A\in\mathscr{D}\mathbb{NPG}_{\mathrm{wrw}}^{\mathrm{wo}}(\Sigma_{\mathrm{wrw}}(\Gamma,\{\bm{1}\}_{\Gamma}))
,
\uplus\alpha\in\mathcal{TS}_{\mathrm{wr}}(\Pi_{\mathrm{wrw}}(\Gamma,A\{\overline{\top_{\Gamma}}\}))
and
\uplus\tau\in\mathcal{TS}_{\mathrm{wr}}(\Pi_{\mathrm{wrw}}(\Gamma,\{\bm{1}\}))
, the equation
\uplus\tau=\top_{\Gamma}
clearly holds, i.e.,
\top
-Uniq is satisfied, and therefore we define
\mathcal{R}^{\bm{1}}_{A}(\uplus\alpha,\uplus\tau)\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\uplus\alpha
.

•

(Unit-Comp)
Clearly,
\mathcal{R}^{\bm{1}}_{A}(\uplus\alpha,\top_{\Gamma})=\uplus\alpha
.

•

(Unit-Subst)
Clearly,
\{\bm{1}\}_{\Gamma}\{\uplus\phi\}=\{\bm{1}\}_{\Delta}
.

•

(
\top
-Subst)
Clearly,
\top_{\Gamma}\bullet\uplus\phi=\top_{\Delta}:\Delta\to\bm{1}
,

which completes the proof.
∎

Finally, we model
empty-type
(Appendix
A.4
).
First, the semantic type former for empty-type is:

Definition 3.51

(CwFs with empty-type
[
20
]
)
.

A CwF
\mathcal{C}

supports empty
if

•

(Empty-Form)
Given
\Gamma\in\mathcal{C}
, there is a type
\bm{0}^{\Gamma}\in\mathrm{Ty}(\Gamma)
, called the
empty-type
in the context
\Gamma
;

•

(Empty-Elim)
Given
\Gamma\in\mathcal{C}
,
A\in\mathrm{Ty}(\Gamma.\bm{0}^{\Gamma})
and
z\in\mathrm{Tm}(\Gamma,\bm{0}^{\Gamma})
, there is a term
\mathcal{R}^{\bm{0}}_{A}(z)\in\mathrm{Tm}(\Gamma,A\{\overline{z}\})
, where
\overline{z}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\langle\mathrm{id}_{\Gamma},z\rangle_{\bm{0}^{\Gamma}}:\Gamma\to\Gamma.\bm{0}^{\Gamma}
;

•

(Empty-Subst)
Given
f:\Delta\to\Gamma
in
\mathcal{C}
,
\bm{0}^{\Gamma}\{f\}=\bm{0}^{\Delta}\in\mathrm{Ty}(\Delta)
;

•

(
\mathcal{R}^{0}
-Subst)

\mathcal{R}^{\bm{0}}_{A\{f^{+}\}}(z\{f\})=\mathcal{R}^{\bm{0}}_{A}(z)\{f\}
, where
f^{+}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\langle f\bullet\mathrm{p}(\bm{0}^{\Delta}),\mathrm{v}_{\bm{0}^{\Gamma}}\rangle_{\bm{0}^{\Gamma}}:\Delta.\bm{0}^{\Delta}\to\Gamma.\bm{0}^{\Gamma}
.

Empty-type is modeled in a CwF that supports empty; see Appendix
A.4
for the details.

Let us finally propose our game-semantic interpretation of empty-type:

Theorem 3.52

(Realizability model of empty-type à la game semantics)
.

The CwF
\mathbb{NPG}_{\mathrm{wrw}}^{\mathrm{wo}}
supports empty.

Proof.

Let
\Gamma,\Delta\in\mathbb{NPG}_{\mathrm{wrw}}^{\mathrm{wo}}
and
\uplus\phi\in\mathbb{NPG}_{\mathrm{wrw}}^{\mathrm{wo}}(\Delta,\Gamma)
.

•

(Empty-Form)

\bm{0}^{\Gamma}
is the constant dependent np-game valued at the empty np-game
\bm{0}
(Example
3.9
), for which we write
\{\bm{0}\}_{\Gamma}
or
\{\bm{0}\}
.

•

(Empty-Elim)
Let
A\in\mathscr{D}\mathbb{NPG}_{\mathrm{wrw}}^{\mathrm{wo}}(\Sigma_{\mathrm{wrw}}(\Gamma,\{\bm{0}\}_{\Gamma}))
and
\uplus\zeta\in\mathcal{TS}_{\mathrm{wr}}(\Pi_{\mathrm{wrw}}(\Gamma,\{\bm{0}\}_{\Gamma}))
.
Because
\mathscr{R}_{\mathrm{wr}}^{\mathrm{cp}}(\bm{0})=\emptyset
, we have
\mathscr{R}_{\mathrm{wr}}^{\mathrm{cp}}(\Gamma)=\emptyset
and
\zeta=\emptyset
.
Hence, it suffices to define
\mathcal{R}^{\bm{0}}_{A}(\uplus\zeta)\in\mathcal{TS}_{\mathrm{wr}}(\Pi_{\mathrm{wrw}}(\Gamma,A\{\overline{\uplus\zeta}\}))
to be the trivial one
\uplus\{\top\}
.

•

(Empty-Subst)
Clearly,
\{\bm{0}\}_{\Gamma}\{\uplus\phi\}=\{\bm{0}\}_{\Delta}
.

•

(
\mathcal{R}^{\bm{0}}
-Subst)
By the definition of the operation
\mathcal{R}^{\bm{0}}_{\_}(\_)
, we calculate

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}^{\bm{0}}_{A\{\uplus\phi^{+}\}}(\uplus\zeta\{\uplus\phi\})

\displaystyle=\uplus\{\top\}

\displaystyle=\uplus\{\top\}\bullet\uplus\phi\quad\text{(n.b., $\mathscr{R}_{\mathrm{wr}}^{\mathrm{cp}}(\Delta)=\emptyset$ and $\phi=\emptyset$)}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{R}^{\bm{0}}_{A\{\uplus\phi^{+}\}}(\uplus\zeta)\bullet\uplus\phi

\displaystyle=\mathcal{R}^{\bm{0}}_{A}(\uplus\zeta)\{\uplus\phi\},

which completes the proof.
∎

3.4
Main result: consistency of intensional Martin-Löf type theory with formal Church’s thesis and its constructivity

Finally, let us solve the targeted open problem, viz., consistency of MLTT with CT,
in the affirmative
.
For precision, we implement the
T-predicate

T
and the
result-extracting function

U
(Section
1.4
) explicitly in MLTT as follows.

We first define a
realizer
for a given partial function
f:\mathbb{N}\rightharpoonup\mathbb{N}
to be a realizer for a t-skeleton
\uplus\phi::\hat{\oc}N\rightarrowtriangle N
whose
extension

\mathrm{ext}(\uplus\phi):n\in\mathbb{N}\mapsto\mathrm{read}(\uplus\phi\bullet\underline{n}_{\bm{1}})
, where
\mathrm{read}(\uplus\{\bot\})\uparrow
and
\mathrm{read}(\underline{m}_{\bm{1}})\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}m
for all
m\in\mathbb{N}
, coincides with
f
.

Then, we implement the T-predicate and the result-extracting function in MLTT with respect to the resulting realizers for total recursive functions:

•

Let the T-predicate to be the type
\mathsf{x:N,y:N,z:N\vdash T(x,y,z)\ type}
defined by
\mathsf{T(\underline{e},\underline{n},\underline{c})\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}1}
if the triple
e,n,c\in\mathbb{N}
satisfies the T-predicate with respect to the realizers just defined in the preceding paragraphs, for which we write
T(e,n,c)
, and
\mathsf{T(\underline{e},\underline{n},\underline{c})\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}0}
otherwise;

•

Let the result-extracting function to be the term
\mathsf{x:N\vdash U(x):N}
defined by
\mathsf{U(\underline{c})\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\underline{n}:N}
if
c\in\mathbb{N}
encodes, with respect to the realizers just defined, the computational history of a terminating computation on
\mathbb{N}
whose output is
n\in\mathbb{N}
, and
\mathsf{U(\underline{c})\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\underline{0}:N}
otherwise.

In practice, the T-predicate and the result-extracting function are given in MLTT by the elimination rule of N-type, which is possible because they are both primitive recursive
[
3
, Section 3.4.3]
.
Thus, having modeled MLTT, including N-type, one may think that a model of the T-predicate and the result-extracting function has been obtained as well.
However, it is not quite the case because we need a
universe
to formalize the T-predicate in that way because it is a type, not a term.

For this point, we could extend our model of MLTT to a universe, but for brevity, let us skip modeling a universe in this article and instead define our interpretation of the T-predicate
directly
:

Definition 3.53

(Realizability model of the T-predicate à la game semantics)
.

Let us interpret the T-predicate in the CwF
\mathbb{NPG}_{\mathrm{wrw}}^{\mathrm{wo}}
by the dependent np-game
\mathcal{T}
on the product
(N\mathbin{\&}N)\mathbin{\&}N
defined by

\mathcal{T}(\langle\langle\uplus\sigma,\uplus\tau\rangle,\uplus\gamma\rangle)\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\begin{cases}\bm{1}&\text{if $T(\mathrm{read}(\uplus\sigma),\mathrm{read}(\uplus\tau),\mathrm{read}(\uplus\gamma))$;}\\
\bm{0}&\text{otherwise.}\end{cases}

Clearly, our interpretation of the T-predicate is
sound
, i.e., it respects the defining judgmental equality.
Having extended our model of MLTT in the CwF
\mathbb{NPG}_{\mathrm{wrw}}^{\mathrm{wo}}
to the T-predicate, we are now ready to present our main theorem:

Theorem 3.54

(Realizability model of MLTT plus CT à la game semantics)
.

The CwF
\mathbb{NPG}_{\mathrm{wrw}}^{\mathrm{wo}}
validates CT, and therefore it models MLTT equipped with CT.

Proof.

We have given a model of MLTT equipped with the T-predicate and unit-, empty-, N-, sigma-, pi- and Id-types in
\mathbb{NPG}_{\mathrm{wrw}}^{\mathrm{wo}}
.
Hence, it remains to validate CT in
\mathbb{NPG}_{\mathrm{wrw}}^{\mathrm{wo}}
.
Let us write
\mathcal{CT}\in\mathbb{NPG}_{\mathrm{wrw}}^{\mathrm{wo}}
for the interpretation of CT (
2
) in
\mathbb{NPG}_{\mathrm{wrw}}^{\mathrm{wo}}
.

As sketched in Section
1.10
, we provide the t-skeleton
\uplus\mathrm{ct}::\mathcal{CT}
whose component
\mathrm{ct}_{(\uplus\phi^{\dagger},e)}
for each
(\uplus\phi^{\dagger},e)\in\mathscr{R}^{\mathrm{cp}}_{\mathrm{wr}}(\hat{\oc}(\hat{\oc}N\rightarrowtriangle N))
is the pairing
\langle\uplus\{\underline{e^{\ddagger}}\},\mathrm{ct}^{\prime}\rangle
up to ‘tags,’ where
e^{\ddagger}\in\pi_{2}(\mathscr{R}^{\mathrm{cp}}_{\mathrm{wr}}(\hat{\oc}N\rightarrowtriangle N))
is the canonical realizer for
\uplus\phi
, and
\mathrm{ct}^{\prime}
is the evident DoWRWLI that essentially computes the code
c\in\mathbb{N}
of the computational history of
\uplus\phi
applied to a given t-skeleton
\underline{n}_{\bm{1}}
(
n\in\mathbb{N}
), so that
T(e^{\ddagger},n,c)
and
\underline{U(c)}_{\bm{1}}=\uplus\phi\bullet\underline{n}_{\bm{1}}
hold.
Finally,
\uplus\mathrm{ct}
is clearly winning and recursive, which completes the proof.
∎

Let us emphasize that our model of MLTT equipped with CT in the CwF
\mathbb{NPG}_{\mathrm{wrw}}^{\mathrm{wo}}
is
constructive
in the sense that every t-skeleton in
\mathbb{NPG}_{\mathrm{wrw}}^{\mathrm{wo}}
is recursive.
In other words, we have established a ‘constructive justification’ of CT within MLTT.

At last, we are now ready to solve the open problem:

Corollary 3.55

(Consistency of MLTT with CT)
.

MLTT is consistent with CT.

Proof.

The corollary follows from Theorem
3.54
because there is no total, let alone winning, t-skeleton on the empty np-game
\bm{0}
.
∎

4
Conclusion and future work

We have proved consistency of MLTT with CT, which has been a long-standing open problem in constructive mathematics, by a novel realizability model of MLTT plus CT à la game semantics.
Our main technical highlight is to resolve the dilemma between intensionality and extensionality in the consistency problem, which existing realizability models cannot overcome, by taking advantage of some distinguishing features of game semantics: the distinction and the asymmetry between O and P.

It is also worth noting that we have validated CT
constructively
in the sense that an ‘effective’ morphism inhabits the interpretation of CT.

Methodologically, the present work has demonstrated that game semantics is a powerful semantic method not only for full abstraction/completeness problems but also for the meta-theoretic study on foundations of constructive mathematics.

As future work, we plan to extend our game semantics of MLTT to
homotopy type theory (HoTT)

[
19
]
and apply the semantics to the study of HoTT similarly to the present work.
We would also like to extend the semantics to other type constructions such as
well-founded tree types

[
15
]
and
induction-recursion

[
51
]
.

Conflict of interest statement

The author states that there is no conflict of interest.

Appendix A
Intensional Martin-Löf type theory

The difference between the
intensional
and the
extensional
variants of MLTT is that there is only the equality in the form of judgements or
judgmental equality
14
14

14

Judgmental equality is modulo
\alpha
-equivalence
or renaming of
bound variables

[
9
,
20
]
.
in the extensional one, while the intensional one in addition has
identity types
for another kind of equality, called
propositional equality
, which is to be witnessed by terms.
See Appendix
A.8
for the details.
It is easy to observe from the rules of identity types that judgmentally equal terms are also propositionally equal, but not vice versa.
Formally, the extensional variant is the intensional one equipped with the axiom of
equality reflection
, which derives judgmental equality from propositional one
[
13
]
(n.b., the two kinds of equalities coincide in the extensional variant).

In this appendix, we briefly review the syntax of the intensional variant, which we call
MLTT
.
We first recall
contexts
in Section
A.1
and
structural rules
in Section
A.2
.
We then recall each type construction in Sections
A.3
-
A.8
.
Along the syntax, we also recall the
interpretation

\llbracket\_\rrbracket
of MLTT in an arbitrary CwF
\mathcal{C}=(\mathcal{C},\mathrm{Ty},\mathrm{Tm},\_\{\_\},T,\_.\_,\mathrm{p},\mathrm{v},\langle\_,\_\rangle_{\_})

[
20
]
fixed throughout this appendix.

Each type construction in MLTT is defined in terms of
formation
,
introduction
,
elimination
and
computation
rules.
Roughly, the formation rule stipulates how to form the type, and the introduction rule defines terms
15
15

15

Strictly speaking, the introduction rule defines
canonical
terms of the type, which in turn defines terms of the type; see
[
14
,
53
,
17
]
on this point.
of the type.
On the other hand, the elimination and the computation rules describe how to consume the terms and the result of the consumption (in the form of equations), respectively, both of which are justified by the introduction rule.

A.1
Contexts

A
context
is a finite sequence
\mathsf{x_{1}:A_{1},x_{2}:A_{2},\dots,x_{n}:A_{n}}
of pairs
(\mathsf{x_{i}},\mathsf{A_{i}})
of a variable
\mathsf{x_{i}}
and a type
\mathsf{A_{i}}
such that the variables
\mathsf{x_{1},x_{2},\dots,x_{n}}
are pairwise distinct.
Let
\mathsf{\diamondsuit}
represent the
empty context
, i.e., the empty sequence
\bm{\epsilon}
.
We often omit contexts (and sometimes the
turnstile

\vdash
) in judgements, especially the empty context
\mathsf{\diamondsuit}
.

We have the following axiom and rules for contexts:

\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\lower 6.50186pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 51.19456pt\hbox{\hskip 15.7778pt\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{}\hskip 4.0pt}}}\vskip-2.49814pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower 1.99812pt\hbox{{{({Ctx-Emp})}}\hskip 3.0pt}\hbox to39.5556pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}}\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{}}\vskip-2.49812pt\hbox{\hskip 51.19456pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\mathsf{\vdash\diamondsuit\ ctx}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}}\ignorespaces\and\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\lower 9.97409pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 46.47234pt\hbox{\>\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\mathsf{\Gamma\vdash A\ type}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}\vskip-2.49814pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower 1.99812pt\hbox{{({Ctx-Ext})}\hskip 3.0pt}\hbox to54.02788pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}}\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{}}\vskip-2.49812pt\hbox{\hskip 46.47234pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\mathsf{\vdash\Gamma,x:A\ ctx}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}}\ignorespaces\and\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\lower 9.97409pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 58.5557pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\mathsf{\vdash\Gamma=\Delta\ ctx}$}\hskip 4.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 14.45377pt}\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\mathsf{\Gamma\vdash A=B\ type\ }$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}\vskip-2.49814pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower 1.99812pt\hbox{{({Ctx-ExtEq})}\hskip 3.0pt}\hbox to136.23175pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}}\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{}}\vskip-2.49812pt\hbox{\hskip 58.5557pt\hbox{\hskip 22.12967pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\mathsf{\vdash\Gamma,x:A=\Delta,y:B\ ctx\ }$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces

where
\mathsf{x}
(resp.
\mathsf{y}
) does not occur in
\mathsf{\Gamma}
(resp.
\mathsf{\Delta}
).

The axiom Ctx-Emp and the rule Ctx-Ext define that contexts are exactly finite lists of pairs of a variable and a type.
On the other hand, the rule Ctx-ExtEq is an instance of a
congruence rule
because it states that judgmental equality
=
on contexts is preserved under ‘context extension’ given by Ctx-Ext.
Note also that we have
\mathsf{\vdash\diamondsuit=\diamondsuit\ ctx}
by Ctx-Emp and the rule Ctx-EqRefl in the next section.

Convention
.

As in
[
20
]
, let us skip writing congruence rules for other constructions.

A.2
Structural rules

Next, let us collect the rules applicable to all types as
structural rules
:

\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\lower 10.00186pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 26.36116pt\hbox{\hskip 0.56113pt\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\mathsf{\vdash x_{1}:A_{1},x_{2}:A_{2},\dots,x_{n}:A_{n}\ ctx}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}\vskip-2.49814pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower 1.99812pt\hbox{{({Var})}\hskip 3.0pt}\hbox to115.13356pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}}\lower 1.99812pt\hbox{\hskip 3.0pt($j\in\{1,2,\dots,n\}$)}}\vskip-2.49812pt\hbox{\hskip 26.36116pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\mathsf{x_{1}:A_{1},x_{2}:A_{2},\dots,x_{n}:A_{n}\vdash x_{j}:A_{j}}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}}\ignorespaces\and\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\lower 9.97409pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 60.22234pt\hbox{\hskip 6.59724pt\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\mathsf{\vdash\Gamma\ ctx}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}\vskip-2.49814pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower 1.99812pt\hbox{{({Ctx-EqRefl})}\hskip 3.0pt}\hbox to50.38898pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}}\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{}}\vskip-2.49812pt\hbox{\hskip 60.22234pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\mathsf{\vdash\Gamma=\Gamma\ ctx}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}}\ignorespaces\and\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\lower 9.97409pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 61.75015pt\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\mathsf{\vdash\Gamma=\Delta\ ctx}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}\vskip-2.49814pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower 1.99812pt\hbox{{({Ctx-EqSym})}\hskip 3.0pt}\hbox to53.30565pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}}\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{}}\vskip-2.49812pt\hbox{\hskip 61.75015pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\mathsf{\vdash\Delta=\Gamma\ ctx}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\and\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\lower 9.97409pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 67.389pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\mathsf{\vdash\Gamma=\Delta\ ctx}$}\hskip 4.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 14.45377pt}\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\mathsf{\vdash\Delta=\Theta\ ctx}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}\vskip-2.49814pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower 1.99812pt\hbox{{({Ctx-EqTrans})}\hskip 3.0pt}\hbox to123.42618pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}}\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{}}\vskip-2.49812pt\hbox{\hskip 67.389pt\hbox{\hskip 35.33804pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\mathsf{\vdash\Gamma=\Theta\ ctx}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\and\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\lower 9.97409pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 56.05565pt\hbox{\hskip 7.22224pt\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\mathsf{\Gamma\vdash A\ type}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}\vskip-2.49814pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower 1.99812pt\hbox{{({Ty-EqRefl})}\hskip 3.0pt}\hbox to63.47234pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}}\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{}}\vskip-2.49812pt\hbox{\hskip 56.05565pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\mathsf{\Gamma\vdash A=A\ type}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}}\ignorespaces\and\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\lower 9.97409pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 57.58347pt\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\mathsf{\Gamma\vdash A=B\ type}$}\hskip 57.58347pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\mathsf{\Gamma\vdash B=A\ type}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\and\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\lower 9.97409pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 63.22232pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\mathsf{\Gamma\vdash A=B\ type}$}\hskip 4.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 14.45377pt}\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\mathsf{\Gamma\vdash B=C\ type}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}\vskip-2.49814pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower 1.99812pt\hbox{{({Ty-EqTrans})}\hskip 3.0pt}\hbox to141.12065pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}}\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{}}\vskip-2.49812pt\hbox{\hskip 63.22232pt\hbox{\hskip 38.96304pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\mathsf{\Gamma\vdash A=C\ type}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\and\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\lower 9.97409pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 59.38899pt\hbox{\hskip 6.29169pt\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\mathsf{\Gamma\vdash a:A}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}\vskip-2.49814pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower 1.99812pt\hbox{{({Tm-EqRefl})}\hskip 3.0pt}\hbox to46.36122pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}}\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{}}\vskip-2.49812pt\hbox{\hskip 59.38899pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\mathsf{\Gamma\vdash a=a:A}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}}\ignorespaces\and\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\lower 10.76875pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 60.91681pt\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\mathsf{\Gamma\vdash a=a^{\prime}:A}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}\vskip-2.49814pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower 1.99812pt\hbox{{({Tm-EqSym})}\hskip 3.0pt}\hbox to49.16678pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}}\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{}}\vskip-2.49812pt\hbox{\hskip 60.91681pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\mathsf{\Gamma\vdash a^{\prime}=a:A}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\and\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\lower 10.76875pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 66.55566pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\mathsf{\Gamma\vdash a=a^{\prime}:A}$}\hskip 4.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 14.45377pt}\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\mathsf{\Gamma\vdash a^{\prime}=a^{\prime\prime}:A}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}\vskip-2.49814pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower 1.99812pt\hbox{{({Tm-EqTrans})}\hskip 3.0pt}\hbox to117.89844pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}}\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{}}\vskip-2.49812pt\hbox{\hskip 66.55566pt\hbox{\hskip 33.21306pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\mathsf{\Gamma\vdash a=a^{\prime\prime}:A}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\and\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\lower 9.97409pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 49.1112pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\mathsf{\vdash\Gamma=\Delta\ ctx}$}\hskip 4.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 14.45377pt}\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\mathsf{\Gamma\vdash A\ type}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}\vskip-2.49814pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower 1.99812pt\hbox{{({Ty-Conv})}\hskip 3.0pt}\hbox to116.78726pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}}\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{}}\vskip-2.49812pt\hbox{\hskip 49.1112pt\hbox{\hskip 32.42137pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\mathsf{\Delta\vdash A\ type}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\and\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\lower 9.97409pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 52.44455pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\mathsf{\Gamma\vdash a:A}$}\hskip 4.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 14.45377pt}\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\mathsf{\vdash\Gamma=\Delta\ ctx}$}\hskip 4.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 14.45377pt}\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\mathsf{\Gamma\vdash A=B\ type}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}\vskip-2.49814pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower 1.99812pt\hbox{{({Tm-Conv})}\hskip 3.0pt}\hbox to179.46336pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}}\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{}}\vskip-2.49812pt\hbox{\hskip 52.44455pt\hbox{\hskip 71.38441pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\mathsf{\Delta\vdash a:B}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces

The rule Var states the reasonable idea that we may give an element
\mathsf{x_{j}:A_{j}}
if it occurs in the context just by ‘copy-catting’ it.
The next nine rules stipulate that every judgmental equality
=
is an equivalence relation.
Finally, the rules Ty-Conv and Tm-Conv formalize the natural phenomenon that judgements are preserved under the exchange of judgmentally equal contexts and/or types.

It is then easy to see that the following
weakening
and
substitution
rules are
admissible
in MLTT, but it is convenient to present them explicitly:

\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\lower 9.97409pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 34.94452pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\mathsf{\Gamma,\Delta\vdash\mathsf{J}}$}\hskip 4.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 14.45377pt}\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\mathsf{\Gamma\vdash A\ type}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}\vskip-2.49814pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower 1.99812pt\hbox{{({Weak})}\hskip 3.0pt}\hbox to98.84279pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}}\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{}}\vskip-2.49812pt\hbox{\hskip 34.94452pt\hbox{\hskip 23.32411pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\mathsf{\Gamma,x:A,\Delta\vdash\mathsf{J}}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\and\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\lower 9.97409pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 35.27786pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\mathsf{\Gamma,x:A,\Delta\vdash\mathsf{J}}$}\hskip 4.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 14.45377pt}\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\mathsf{\Gamma\vdash a:A}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}\vskip-2.49814pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower 1.99812pt\hbox{{({Subst})}\hskip 3.0pt}\hbox to100.42618pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}}\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{}}\vskip-2.49812pt\hbox{\hskip 35.27786pt\hbox{\hskip 12.56021pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\mathsf{\Gamma,\Delta[a/x]\vdash\mathsf{J}[a/x]}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces

where
\mathsf{x}
does not occur in
\mathsf{\Gamma}
or
\mathsf{\Delta}
for Weak, and not in
\mathsf{\Gamma}
for Subst, and
\mathsf{\mathsf{J}[a/x]}
(resp.
\mathsf{\Delta[a/x]}
) is the
capture-free substitution

[
54
]
of
\mathsf{a}
for
\mathsf{x}
in
\mathsf{J}
16
16

16

Here,
\mathsf{J}
denotes the RHS of the turnstile
\vdash
in an arbitrary judgement.
(resp.
\Delta
).

Note that a priori we cannot define an interpretation of MLTT by induction on deductions since a derivation of a judgement in MLTT is not unique in the presence of the rules Ty-Con and Tm-Con
[
20
]
.
For this point, a standard approach is to define an interpretation
\llbracket\_\rrbracket
on
pre-syntax
, which is
partial
, by induction on the length of pre-syntax, and show that it is well-defined on every ‘valid pre-syntax,’ i.e., judgement, and preserves judgmental equality by the semantic equality.
By this
soundness theorem

[
20
]
, a posteriori we may describe the interpretation
\llbracket\_\rrbracket
of the syntax by induction on derivation of judgements:

Definition A.1

(Interpretation of contexts and structural rules in CwFs
[
20
]
)
.

The interpretation
\llbracket\_\rrbracket
of contexts and structural rules in a CwF
\mathcal{C}
is defined by:

•

(
Ct-Emp
)
\llbracket\mathsf{\vdash\diamondsuit\ ctx}\rrbracket\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}T
;

•

(
Ct-Ext
)
\llbracket\mathsf{\vdash\Gamma,x:A\ ctx}\rrbracket\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\llbracket\mathsf{\vdash\Gamma\ ctx}\rrbracket.\llbracket\mathsf{\Gamma\vdash A\ type}\rrbracket
;

•

(
Var
)
\llbracket\mathsf{\Gamma,x:A\vdash x:A}\rrbracket\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\mathrm{v}_{\llbracket\mathsf{A}\rrbracket}
;

\llbracket\mathsf{\Gamma,x:A,\Delta,y:B\vdash x:A}\rrbracket\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\llbracket\mathsf{\Gamma,x:A,\Delta\vdash x:A}\rrbracket\{\mathrm{p}(\llbracket\mathsf{\Gamma,x:A,\Delta\vdash B\ type}\rrbracket)\}_{\llbracket\mathsf{A}\rrbracket}
;

•

(
Ty-Con
)
\llbracket\mathsf{\Delta\vdash A\ type}\rrbracket\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\llbracket\mathsf{\Gamma\vdash A\ type}\rrbracket
;

•

(
Tm-Con
)
\llbracket\mathsf{\Delta\vdash a:B}\rrbracket\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\llbracket\mathsf{\Gamma\vdash a:A}\rrbracket
.

In the rest of this appendix, we recall specific type constructions in MLTT and the interpretation of them in an arbitrary CwF.

A.3
Unit-type

Let us begin with the simplest type, called
unit-type
(or
one-type
)
\mathsf{1}
, which is the type that has just one term
\mathsf{\top}
.
17
17

17

Strictly speaking,
\mathsf{1}
has just one
canonical
term
\mathsf{\top}
. However, for simplicity, let us be casual about the distinction between canonical and non-canonical terms in the present work, and we usually call canonical/non-canonical terms just
terms
.

Therefore, from the logical point of view, it represents the ‘simplest true formula.’

Rules of unit-type are the following:

\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\lower 9.97409pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 42.05565pt\hbox{\enskip\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\mathsf{\vdash\Gamma\ ctx}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}\vskip-2.49814pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower 1.99812pt\hbox{{({$\mathsf{1}$-Form})}\hskip 3.0pt}\hbox to47.36118pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}}\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{}}\vskip-2.49812pt\hbox{\hskip 42.05565pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\mathsf{\Gamma\vdash 1\ type}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}}\ignorespaces\and\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\lower 9.97409pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 40.80563pt\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\mathsf{\vdash\Gamma\ ctx}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}\vskip-2.49814pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower 1.99812pt\hbox{{({$\mathsf{1}$-Intro})}\hskip 3.0pt}\hbox to37.19449pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}}\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{}}\vskip-2.49812pt\hbox{\hskip 40.80563pt\hbox{\hskip 0.80554pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\mathsf{\Gamma\vdash\top:\bm{1}}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\and\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\lower 9.97409pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 40.2223pt\hbox{\hskip 7.7778pt\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\mathsf{\Gamma\vdash t:1}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}\vskip-2.49814pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower 1.99812pt\hbox{{({$\mathsf{1}$-Uniq})}\hskip 3.0pt}\hbox to46.47232pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}}\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{}}\vskip-2.49812pt\hbox{\hskip 40.2223pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\mathsf{\Gamma\vdash t=\top:1}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}}\ignorespaces\and\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\lower 11.24594pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 39.80565pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\mathsf{\Gamma,x:\bm{1}\vdash C\ type}$}\hskip 4.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 14.45377pt}\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\mathsf{\Gamma\vdash c:C[\top/x]}$}\hskip 4.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 14.45377pt}\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\mathsf{\Gamma\vdash t:\bm{1}}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}\vskip-2.49814pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower 1.99812pt\hbox{{({$\mathsf{1}$-Elim})}\hskip 3.0pt}\hbox to180.82451pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}}\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{}}\vskip-2.49812pt\hbox{\hskip 39.80565pt\hbox{\hskip 47.38298pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\mathsf{\Gamma\vdash R^{\bm{1}}(C,c,t):C[t/x]}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\and\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\lower 11.24594pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 45.22232pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\mathsf{\Gamma,x:\bm{1}\vdash C\ type}$}\hskip 4.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 14.45377pt}\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\mathsf{\Gamma\vdash c:C[\top/x]}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}\vskip-2.49814pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower 1.99812pt\hbox{{({$\mathsf{1}$-Comp})}\hskip 3.0pt}\hbox to134.95401pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}}\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{}}\vskip-2.49812pt\hbox{\hskip 45.22232pt\hbox{\hskip 14.16994pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\mathsf{\Gamma\vdash R^{\bm{1}}(C,c,\top)=c:C[\top/x]}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces

Note that
\mathsf{1}
-Uniq implies
\mathsf{1}
-Elim and
\mathsf{1}
-Comp if we define
\mathsf{R^{1}(C,c,t)\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}c}
.

The formation rule
\mathsf{1}
-Form states that unit-type is
atomic
, i.e., we may form it without assuming any other types.
The introduction rule
\mathsf{1}
-Intro defines that it has just one term, viz.,
\mathsf{\top}
.
Then, the uniqueness rule
\mathsf{1}
-Uniq makes sense, from which the remaining rules
\mathsf{1}
-Elim and
\mathsf{1}
-Comp follow.

Definition A.2

(Interpretation of unit-type in CwFs)
.

The interpretation
\llbracket\_\rrbracket
of unit-type in a CwF
\mathcal{C}
that supports unit is given by:

•

(
\mathsf{1}
-Form
)
\llbracket\mathsf{\Gamma\vdash 1\ type}\rrbracket\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\bm{1}^{\llbracket\mathsf{\Gamma}\rrbracket}
;

•

(
\mathsf{1}
-Intro
)
\llbracket\mathsf{\Gamma\vdash\top:1}\rrbracket\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\top_{\llbracket\mathsf{\Gamma}\rrbracket}
;

•

(
\mathsf{1}
-Elim
)
\llbracket\mathsf{\Gamma\vdash R^{1}(C,c,t):C[t/x]}\rrbracket\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\mathcal{R}^{\bm{1}}_{\llbracket\mathsf{C}\rrbracket}(\llbracket\mathsf{c}\rrbracket,\llbracket\mathsf{t}\rrbracket)
.

A.4
Empty-type

Next, let us recall
empty-type
(or
zero-type
)
\mathsf{0}
, which is the type that has no terms.
Thus, it corresponds in logic to the ‘simplest false formula.’

Rules of empty-type are the following:

\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\lower 9.97409pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 42.05565pt\hbox{\hskip 5.33334pt\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\mathsf{\vdash\Gamma\ ctx}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}\vskip-2.49814pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower 1.99812pt\hbox{{({$\mathsf{0}$-Form})}\hskip 3.0pt}\hbox to47.86119pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}}\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{}}\vskip-2.49812pt\hbox{\hskip 42.05565pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\mathsf{\Gamma\vdash\bm{0}\ type}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}}\ignorespaces\and\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\lower 10.96815pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 39.80565pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\mathsf{\Gamma,x:\bm{0}\vdash C\ type}$}\hskip 4.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 14.45377pt}\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\mathsf{\Gamma\vdash a:\bm{0}}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}\vskip-2.49814pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower 1.99812pt\hbox{{({$\mathsf{0}$-Elim})}\hskip 3.0pt}\hbox to111.48175pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}}\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{}}\vskip-2.49812pt\hbox{\hskip 39.80565pt\hbox{\hskip 15.12827pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\mathsf{\Gamma\vdash R^{\bm{0}}(C,a):C[a/x]}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces

The formation rule
\mathsf{0}
-Form is similar to
\mathsf{1}
-Form, and the elimination rule
\mathsf{0}
-Elim corresponds in logic to
ex falso
, i.e., ‘anything follows from a contradiction.’
Empty-type has neither introduction nor computation rule since it has no terms.

Definition A.3

(Interpretation of empty-type in CwFs)
.

The interpretation
\llbracket\_\rrbracket
of empty-type in a CwF
\mathcal{C}
that supports empty is given by:

•

(
\mathsf{0}
-Form
)
\llbracket\mathsf{\Gamma\vdash 0\ type}\rrbracket\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\bm{0}^{\llbracket\mathsf{\Gamma}\rrbracket}
;

•

(
\mathsf{0}
-Elim
)
\llbracket\mathsf{\Gamma\vdash R^{0}(C,a):C[a/x]}\rrbracket\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\mathcal{R}^{\bm{0}}_{\llbracket\mathsf{C}\rrbracket}(\llbracket\mathsf{a}\rrbracket)
.

A.5
N-type

We proceed to recall an atomic type of computational significance,
natural number type
(or
N-type
)
\mathsf{N}
, which is a type of natural numbers.

Rules of N-type are the following:

\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\lower 10.27965pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 38.63522pt\hbox{\hskip 5.19452pt\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\mathsf{\vdash\Gamma\ ctx}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}\vskip-1.49814pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower 1.49812pt\hbox{{({$\mathsf{N}$-Form})}\hskip 3.0pt}\hbox to43.22272pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}}\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{}}\vskip-1.49812pt\hbox{\hskip 38.63522pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\mathsf{\Gamma\vdash N\ type}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}}\ignorespaces\and\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\lower 10.27965pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 42.29083pt\hbox{\hskip 3.98616pt\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\mathsf{\vdash\Gamma\ ctx}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}\vskip-1.49814pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower 1.49812pt\hbox{{({$\mathsf{N}$-IntroZ})}\hskip 3.0pt}\hbox to40.80602pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}}\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{}}\vskip-1.49812pt\hbox{\hskip 42.29083pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\mathsf{\Gamma\vdash zero:N}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}}\ignorespaces\and\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\lower 10.27965pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 41.8186pt\hbox{\hskip 10.90709pt\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\mathsf{\Gamma\vdash n:N}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}\vskip-1.49814pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower 1.49812pt\hbox{{({$\mathsf{N}$-IntroS})}\hskip 3.0pt}\hbox to52.38396pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}}\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{}}\vskip-1.49812pt\hbox{\hskip 41.8186pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\mathsf{\Gamma\vdash succ(n):N}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}}\ignorespaces\and\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\lower 10.50186pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 36.03102pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\mathsf{\Gamma,x:N\vdash C\ type}$}\hskip 4.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 14.45377pt}\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\mathsf{\Gamma\vdash c_{z}:C[zero/x]}$}\hskip 4.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 14.45377pt}\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\mathsf{\Gamma,x:N,y:C\vdash c_{s}:C[succ(x)/x]}$}\hskip 4.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 14.45377pt}\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\mathsf{\Gamma\vdash n:N}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}\vskip-1.49814pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower 1.49812pt\hbox{{({$\mathsf{N}$-Elim})}\hskip 3.0pt}\hbox to290.42592pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}}\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{}}\vskip-1.49812pt\hbox{\hskip 36.03102pt\hbox{\hskip 100.45938pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\mathsf{\Gamma\vdash R^{N}(C,c_{z},c_{s},n):C[n/x]}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\and\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\lower 10.50186pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 45.83394pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\mathsf{\Gamma,x:N\vdash C\ type}$}\hskip 4.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 14.45377pt}\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\mathsf{\Gamma\vdash c_{z}:C[zero/x]}$}\hskip 4.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 14.45377pt}\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\mathsf{\Gamma,x:N,y:C\vdash c_{s}:C[succ(x)/x]}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}\vskip-1.49814pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower 1.49812pt\hbox{{({$\mathsf{N}$-CompZ})}\hskip 3.0pt}\hbox to245.40239pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}}\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{}}\vskip-1.49812pt\hbox{\hskip 45.83394pt\hbox{\hskip 61.04962pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\mathsf{\Gamma\vdash R^{N}(C,c_{z},c_{s},zero)=c_{z}:C[zero/x]}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\and\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\lower 10.50186pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 45.36171pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\mathsf{\Gamma,x:N\vdash C\ type}$}\hskip 4.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 14.45377pt}\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\mathsf{\Gamma\vdash c_{z}:C[zero/x]}$}\hskip 4.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 14.45377pt}\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\mathsf{\Gamma,x:N,y:C\vdash c_{s}:C[succ(x)/x]}$}\hskip 4.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 14.45377pt}\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\mathsf{\Gamma\vdash n:N}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}\vskip-1.49814pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower 1.49812pt\hbox{{({$\mathsf{N}$-CompS})}\hskip 3.0pt}\hbox to290.42592pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}}\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{}}\vskip-1.49812pt\hbox{\hskip 45.36171pt\hbox{\hskip 34.91548pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\mathsf{\Gamma\vdash R^{N}(C,c_{z},c_{s},succ(n))=c_{s}[n/x,R^{N}(C,c_{z},c_{s},n)/y]:C[succ(n)/x]}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces

Again, the formation rule
\mathsf{N}
-Form says that N-type is atomic.
The introduction rules
\mathsf{N}
-IntroZ and
\mathsf{N}
-IntroZ inductively define terms of N-type:
\mathsf{zero}
(for
0\in\mathbb{N}
) and
\mathsf{succ(n)}
if so is
\mathsf{n}
(for
n\in\mathbb{N}\Rightarrow n+1\in\mathbb{N}
).
The elimination rule
\mathsf{N}
-Elim represents both
mathematical induction
and
primitive recursion
: To show a predicate
\mathsf{C}
over
\mathsf{N}
, it suffices to prove
\mathsf{C(zero)}
and
\mathsf{C(n)}\Rightarrow\mathsf{C(succ(n))}
, or equivalently under the CHIs, to define a (dependent) function
\mathsf{f}
from
\mathsf{N}
to
\mathsf{C}
, it suffices to define its outputs
\mathsf{f(zero)}
on
\mathsf{zero}
and
\mathsf{f(succ(n))}
on
\mathsf{succ(n)}
in terms of
\mathsf{f(n)}
and
\mathsf{n}
.
The elimination rule makes sense by the introduction rule, i.e., for terms of N-type are only zero and successors.
Finally, the computation rules
\mathsf{N}
-CompZ and
\mathsf{N}
-CompS stipulate the expected behavior of computations given by
\mathsf{N}
-Elim.

Notation
.

Given a context
\mathsf{\vdash\Gamma\ ctx}
and a natural number
n\in\mathbb{N}
, we define the term
\mathsf{\Gamma\vdash\underline{n}:N}
, called the
\bm{n}^{\text{th}}
numeral
(in the context
\Gamma
), by induction on
n
:
\mathsf{\underline{0}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}zero}
and
\mathsf{\underline{n+1}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}succ(\underline{n})}
.
That is, the
n^{\text{th}}
numeral is to represent the number
n
.

Definition A.4

(Interpretation of N-type in CwFs)
.

The interpretation
\llbracket\_\rrbracket
of N-type in a CwF
\mathcal{C}
that supports N is given by:

•

(
\mathsf{N}
-Form
)
\llbracket\mathsf{\Gamma\vdash N\ type}\rrbracket\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}N^{\llbracket\mathsf{\Gamma}\rrbracket}
;

•

(
\mathsf{N}
-IntroZ
)
\llbracket\mathsf{\Gamma\vdash zero:N}\rrbracket\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\underline{0}_{\llbracket\mathsf{\Gamma}\rrbracket}
;

•

(
\mathsf{N}
-IntroS
)
\llbracket\mathsf{\Gamma\vdash succ(n):N}\rrbracket\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\mathrm{v}_{N}\{\mathrm{succ}_{\llbracket\mathsf{\Gamma}\rrbracket}\circ\langle\mathrm{id}_{\llbracket\mathsf{\Gamma}\rrbracket},\llbracket\mathsf{\Gamma\vdash n:N}\rrbracket\rangle\}
;

•

(
\mathsf{N}
-Elim
)
\llbracket\mathsf{\Gamma\vdash R^{N}(C,c_{z},c_{s},n):C[n/x]}\rrbracket\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\mathcal{R}^{N}_{\llbracket\mathsf{C}\rrbracket}(\llbracket\mathsf{c_{z}}\rrbracket,\llbracket\mathsf{c_{s}}\rrbracket)\{\langle\mathrm{id}_{\llbracket\mathsf{\Gamma}\rrbracket},\llbracket\mathsf{n}\rrbracket\rangle_{N}\}
.

It is easy to see by (the meta-theoretic) mathematical induction that the equation
\llbracket\mathsf{\Gamma\vdash\underline{n}:N}\rrbracket=\underline{n}_{\llbracket\mathsf{\Gamma}\rrbracket}
holds for any context
\mathsf{\vdash\Gamma\ ctx}
and natural number
n\in\mathbb{N}
.

A.6
Pi-types

Now, let us recall the
dependent function types
(or
pi-types
) construction
\mathsf{\Pi}
.
In terms of a set-theoretic analogy, the pi-type
\mathsf{\Pi_{x:A}B(x)}
is something like the set of all (total) functions
\mathsf{f}
from
\mathsf{A}
to
\bigcup_{\mathsf{x:A}}\mathsf{B(x)}
such that
\mathsf{f(a):B(a)}
for all
\mathsf{a:A}
, called
dependent functions
from
\mathsf{A}
to
\mathsf{B}
, where we informally interpret simple types
\mathsf{A}
and terms
\mathsf{a:A}
as sets and elements
\mathsf{a\in A}
of sets, respectively, and dependent types
\mathsf{B}
on
\mathsf{A}
as families
\mathsf{(B(x))_{x:A}}
of sets
\mathsf{B(x)}
.

Rules of pi-types are the following:

\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\lower 10.12686pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 41.03723pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\mathsf{\Gamma\vdash A\ type}$}\hskip 4.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 14.45377pt}\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\mathsf{\Gamma,x:A\vdash B\ type}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}\vskip-1.99814pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower 1.74812pt\hbox{{({$\mathsf{\Pi}$-Form})}\hskip 3.0pt}\hbox to122.01556pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}}\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{}}\vskip-1.99812pt\hbox{\hskip 41.03723pt\hbox{\hskip 29.83324pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\mathsf{\Gamma\vdash\Pi_{x:A}B\ type}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\and\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\lower 10.72508pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 39.8803pt\hbox{\hskip 8.85457pt\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\mathsf{\Gamma,x:A\vdash b:B}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}\vskip-1.99814pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower 1.74812pt\hbox{{({$\mathsf{\Pi}$-Intro})}\hskip 3.0pt}\hbox to65.50743pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}}\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{}}\vskip-1.99812pt\hbox{\hskip 39.8803pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\mathsf{\Gamma\vdash\lambda x^{A}.b:\Pi_{x:A}B}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}}\ignorespaces\and\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\lower 10.12686pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 38.96086pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\mathsf{\Gamma\vdash f:\Pi_{x:A}B}$}\hskip 4.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 14.45377pt}\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\mathsf{\Gamma\vdash a:A}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}\vskip-1.99814pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower 1.74812pt\hbox{{({$\mathsf{\Pi}$-Elim})}\hskip 3.0pt}\hbox to92.96948pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}}\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{}}\vskip-1.99812pt\hbox{\hskip 38.96086pt\hbox{\hskip 16.29744pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\mathsf{\Gamma\vdash f(a):B[a/x]}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\and\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\lower 10.72508pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 43.97195pt\hbox{\hskip 11.37643pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\mathsf{\Gamma,x:A\vdash b:B}$}\hskip 4.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 14.45377pt}\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\mathsf{\Gamma\vdash a:A}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}\vskip-1.99814pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower 1.74812pt\hbox{{({$\mathsf{\Pi}$-Comp})}\hskip 3.0pt}\hbox to116.89365pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}}\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{}}\vskip-1.99812pt\hbox{\hskip 43.97195pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\mathsf{\Gamma\vdash(\lambda x^{A}.b)(a)=b[a/x]:B[a/x]}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}}\ignorespaces\and\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\lower 10.72508pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 39.34558pt\hbox{\hskip 19.20404pt\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\mathsf{\Gamma\vdash f:\Pi_{x:A}B}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}\vskip-1.99814pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower 1.74812pt\hbox{{({$\mathsf{\Pi}$-Uniq})}\hskip 3.0pt}\hbox to85.03508pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}}\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{}}\vskip-1.99812pt\hbox{\hskip 39.34558pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\mathsf{\Gamma\vdash\lambda x^{A}.f(x)=f:\Pi_{x:A}B}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}}\ignorespaces

where in
\mathsf{\Pi}
-Uniq
the variable
\mathsf{x}
does not occur free in
\mathsf{f}
.

The formation rule
\mathsf{\Pi}
-Form states that we may form the pi-type
\mathsf{\Pi_{x:A}B}
from types
\mathsf{A}
and
\mathsf{B}
, where
\mathsf{B}
depends on
\mathsf{A}
.
The introduction rule
\mathsf{\Pi}
-Intro defines how to construct terms of
\mathsf{\Pi_{x:A}B}
; it is the ordinary
currying
yet generalized to dependent types.
Then, the elimination and the computation rules
\mathsf{\Pi}
-Elim and
\mathsf{\Pi}
-Comp make sense by the introduction rule.
Finally, the uniqueness rule
\mathsf{\Pi}
-Uniq stipulates that (canonical) terms of pi-types are only
\lambda
-abstractions.

Definition A.5

(Interpretation of pi-types in CwFs
[
20
]
)
.

The interpretation
\llbracket\_\rrbracket
of pi-types in a CwF
\mathcal{C}
that supports pi is given by:

•

(
\mathsf{\Pi}
-Form
)
\llbracket\mathsf{\Gamma\vdash\Pi_{x:A}B\ type}\rrbracket\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\Pi(\llbracket\mathsf{\Gamma\vdash A\ type}\rrbracket,\llbracket\mathsf{\Gamma,x:A\vdash B\ type}\rrbracket)
;

•

(
\mathsf{\Pi}
-Intro
)
\llbracket\mathsf{\Gamma\vdash\lambda x.\ \!b:\Pi_{x:A}B}\rrbracket\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\lambda_{\llbracket\mathsf{A}\rrbracket,\llbracket\mathsf{B}\rrbracket}(\llbracket\mathsf{\Gamma,x:A\vdash b:B}\rrbracket)
;

•

(
\mathsf{\Pi}
-Elim
)
\llbracket\mathsf{\Gamma\vdash f(a):B[a/x]}\rrbracket\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\mathrm{App}_{\llbracket\mathsf{A}\rrbracket,\llbracket\mathsf{B}\rrbracket}(\llbracket\mathsf{\Gamma\vdash f:\Pi_{x:A}B}\rrbracket,\llbracket\mathsf{\Gamma\vdash a:A}\rrbracket)
.

A.7
Sigma-types

Another important type construction is the
dependent sum types
(or
sigma-types
) construction
\mathsf{\Sigma}
.
In terms of the set-theoretic analogy again, the sigma-type
\mathsf{\Sigma_{x:A}B(x)}
represents the set of all pairs
\mathsf{\langle a,b\rangle}
such that
\mathsf{a:A}
and
\mathsf{b:B(a)}
, called
dependent pairs
of
\mathsf{A}
and
\mathsf{B}
.

Rules of sigma-types are the following:

\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\lower 10.12686pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 41.17612pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\mathsf{\Gamma\vdash A\ type}$}\hskip 4.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 14.45377pt}\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\mathsf{\Gamma,x:A\vdash B\ type}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}\vskip-1.99814pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower 1.74812pt\hbox{{({$\mathsf{\Sigma}$-Form})}\hskip 3.0pt}\hbox to122.01556pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}}\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{}}\vskip-1.99812pt\hbox{\hskip 41.17612pt\hbox{\hskip 29.7638pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\mathsf{\Gamma\vdash\Sigma_{x:A}B\ type}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\and\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\lower 10.37686pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 40.01918pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\mathsf{\Gamma,x:A\vdash B\ type}$}\hskip 4.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 14.45377pt}\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\mathsf{\Gamma\vdash a:A}$}\hskip 4.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 14.45377pt}\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\mathsf{\Gamma\vdash b:B[a/x]}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}\vskip-1.99814pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower 1.74812pt\hbox{{({$\mathsf{\Sigma}$-Intro})}\hskip 3.0pt}\hbox to173.05244pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}}\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{}}\vskip-1.99812pt\hbox{\hskip 40.01918pt\hbox{\hskip 54.77177pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\mathsf{\Gamma\vdash\langle a,b\rangle:\Sigma_{x:A}B}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\and\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\lower 11.35008pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 39.09975pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\mathsf{\Gamma,z:\Sigma_{x:A}B\vdash C\ type}$}\hskip 4.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 14.45377pt}\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\mathsf{\Gamma,x:A,y:B\vdash g:C[\langle x,y\rangle/z]}$}\hskip 4.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 14.45377pt}\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\mathsf{\Gamma\vdash p:\Sigma_{x:A}B}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}\vskip-1.99814pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower 1.74812pt\hbox{{({$\mathsf{\Sigma}$-Elim})}\hskip 3.0pt}\hbox to250.79953pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}}\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{}}\vskip-1.99812pt\hbox{\hskip 39.09975pt\hbox{\hskip 49.1207pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\mathsf{\Gamma\vdash R^{\Sigma}([z:\Sigma_{x:A}B]C,[x:A,y:B]g,p):C[p/z]}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\and\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\lower 11.35008pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 44.11084pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\mathsf{\Gamma,z:\Sigma_{x:A}B\vdash C\ type}$}\hskip 4.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 14.45377pt}\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\mathsf{\Gamma,x:A,y:B\vdash g:C[\langle x,y\rangle/z]}$}\hskip 4.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 14.45377pt}\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\mathsf{\Gamma\vdash a:A}$}\hskip 4.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 14.45377pt}\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\mathsf{\Gamma\vdash b:B[a/x]}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}\vskip-1.99814pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower 1.74812pt\hbox{{({$\mathsf{\Sigma}$-Comp})}\hskip 3.0pt}\hbox to299.11194pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}}\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{}}\vskip-1.99812pt\hbox{\hskip 44.11084pt\hbox{\hskip 35.22156pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\mathsf{\Gamma\vdash R^{\Sigma}([z:\Sigma_{x:A}B]C,[x:A,y:B]g,\langle a,b\rangle)=g[a/x,b/y]:C[\langle a,b\rangle/z]}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\and\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\lower 10.72508pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 39.48447pt\hbox{\hskip 37.15364pt\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\mathsf{\Gamma\vdash p:\Sigma_{x:A}B}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}\vskip-1.99814pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower 1.74812pt\hbox{{({$\mathsf{\Sigma}$-Uniq})}\hskip 3.0pt}\hbox to123.02452pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}}\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{}}\vskip-1.99812pt\hbox{\hskip 39.48447pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\mathsf{\Gamma\vdash\langle\pi^{A,B}_{1}(p),\pi^{A,B}_{2}(p)\rangle=p:\Sigma_{x:A}B}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}}\ignorespaces

where

\displaystyle\mathsf{\Gamma\vdash\pi^{A,B}_{1}(p)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{df.}}}{{\equiv}}R^{\Sigma}([z:\Sigma_{x:A}B]A,[x:A,y:B]x,p):A}

\displaystyle\mathsf{\Gamma\vdash\pi^{A,B}_{2}(p)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathrm{df.}}}{{\equiv}}R^{\Sigma}([z:\Sigma_{x:A}B]B[\pi^{A,B}_{1}(z)/x],[x:A,y:B]y,p]):B[\pi^{A,B}_{1}(p)/x]}

are
projections
constructed by
\mathsf{\Sigma}
-Elim
.

The formation rule
\mathsf{\Sigma}
-Form is the same as that of pi-types.
The introduction rule
\mathsf{\Sigma}
-Intro specifies that terms of a sigma-type
\mathsf{\Sigma_{x:A}B}
are dependent pairs
\mathsf{\langle a,b\rangle:\Sigma_{x:A}B}
of terms
\mathsf{a:A}
and
\mathsf{b:B[a/x]}
.
Again, the elimination and the computation rules
\mathsf{\Sigma}
-Elim and
\mathsf{\Sigma}
-Comp make sense by the introduction rule.
Finally, the uniqueness rule
\mathsf{\Sigma}
-Uniq stipulates that (canonical) terms of sigma-types are only dependent pairs.

Definition A.6

(Interpretation of sigma-types in CwFs
[
20
]
)
.

The interpretation
\llbracket\_\rrbracket
of sigma-types in a CwF
\mathcal{C}
that supports sigma is given by:

•

(
\Sigma
-Form
)
\llbracket\mathsf{\Gamma\vdash\Sigma_{x:A}B\ type}\rrbracket\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\Sigma(\llbracket\mathsf{\Gamma\vdash A\ type}\rrbracket,\llbracket\mathsf{\Gamma,x:A\vdash B\ type}\rrbracket)
;

•

(
\Sigma
-Intro
)
\llbracket\mathsf{\Gamma\vdash(a,b):\Sigma_{x:A}B}\rrbracket\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\mathrm{Pair}_{\llbracket\mathsf{A}\rrbracket,\llbracket\mathsf{B}\rrbracket}\circ\langle\overline{\llbracket\mathsf{\Gamma\vdash a:A}\rrbracket},\llbracket\mathsf{\Gamma\vdash b:B[a/x]\rrbracket}\rangle_{\llbracket\mathsf{B}\rrbracket}
;

•

(
\Sigma
-Elim
)
\llbracket\mathsf{\Gamma\vdash R^{\Sigma}(C,g,p):C[p/z]}\rrbracket\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\mathcal{R}^{\Sigma}_{\llbracket\mathsf{A}\rrbracket,\llbracket\mathsf{B}\rrbracket,\llbracket\mathsf{C}\rrbracket}(\llbracket\mathsf{\Gamma,x:A,y:B\vdash g:C[(x,y)/z]}\rrbracket)\circ\overline{\llbracket\mathsf{\Gamma\vdash p:\Sigma_{x:A}B}\rrbracket}
,

where
\overline{\llbracket\mathsf{\Gamma\vdash a:A}\rrbracket}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\langle\mathit{id}_{\llbracket\mathsf{\Gamma}\rrbracket},\llbracket\mathsf{a}\rrbracket\rangle:\llbracket\mathsf{\Gamma}\rrbracket\to\llbracket\mathsf{\Gamma}\rrbracket.\llbracket\mathsf{A}\rrbracket
and
\overline{\llbracket\mathsf{\Gamma\vdash p:\Sigma_{x:A}B}\rrbracket}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\langle\mathit{id}_{\llbracket\mathsf{\Gamma}\rrbracket},\llbracket\mathsf{p}\rrbracket\rangle:\llbracket\mathsf{\Gamma}\rrbracket\to\llbracket\mathsf{\Gamma}\rrbracket.\llbracket\mathsf{\Sigma_{x:A}B}\rrbracket
.

A.8
Id-types

Note that a judgmental equality
\mathsf{\Gamma\vdash a=a^{\prime}:A}
is a judgement, not a formula, and therefore it cannot be used in a context or derived by an induction principle such as
\mathsf{N}
-Elim.
To overcome this deficiency, the
identity types
(or
Id-types
) construction
\mathrm{Id}
has been introduced.
18
18

18

We can then, e.g., formulate and prove
Peano axioms

[
55
]
by Id-types.

Informally, the Id-type
\mathsf{Id_{A}(a,a^{\prime})}
represents the set of all
(identity) proofs
that ‘witnesses’ equality between
\mathsf{a}
and
\mathsf{a^{\prime}}
.

Rules of Id-types are the following:

\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\lower 10.66351pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 42.97331pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\mathsf{\Gamma\vdash A\ type}$}\hskip 4.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 14.45377pt}\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\mathsf{\Gamma\vdash a:A}$}\hskip 4.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 14.45377pt}\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\mathsf{\Gamma\vdash a^{\prime}:A}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}\vskip-1.99814pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower 1.74812pt\hbox{{({$\mathrm{Id}$-Form})}\hskip 3.0pt}\hbox to141.48189pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}}\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{}}\vskip-1.99812pt\hbox{\hskip 42.97331pt\hbox{\hskip 34.07588pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\mathsf{\Gamma\vdash Id_{A}(a,a^{\prime})\ type}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\and\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\lower 10.12686pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 41.81638pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\mathsf{\Gamma\vdash A\ type}$}\hskip 4.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 14.45377pt}\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\mathsf{\Gamma\vdash a:A}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}\vskip-1.99814pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower 1.74812pt\hbox{{({$\mathrm{Id}$-Intro})}\hskip 3.0pt}\hbox to92.33525pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}}\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{}}\vskip-1.99812pt\hbox{\hskip 41.81638pt\hbox{\hskip 12.26239pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\mathsf{\Gamma\vdash refl_{a}:Id_{A}(a,a)}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces\and\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\lower 10.66351pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 40.89694pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\mathsf{\Gamma,x:A,y:A,p:Id_{A}(x,y)\vdash C\ type}$}\hskip 4.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 14.45377pt}\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\mathsf{\Gamma,z:A\vdash c:C[z/x,z/y,refl_{z}/p]}$}\hskip 4.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 14.45377pt}\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\mathsf{\Gamma\vdash q:Id_{A}(a,a^{\prime})}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}\vskip-1.99814pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower 10.37686pt\hbox{\vbox{\hbox{\hskip 45.90804pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\mathsf{\Gamma,x:A,y:A,p:Id_{A}(x,y)\vdash C\ type}$}\hskip 4.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 14.45377pt}\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\mathsf{\Gamma,z:A\vdash c:C[z/x,z/y,refl_{z}/p]}$}\hskip 4.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 14.45377pt}\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\mathsf{\Gamma\vdash a:A}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}\vskip-1.99814pt\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower 1.74812pt\hbox{{({$\mathrm{Id}$-Comp})}\hskip 3.0pt}\hbox to284.85147pt{\leaders{\hrule}{\hfill}}\lower-0.50186pt\hbox{}}\vskip-1.99812pt\hbox{\hskip 45.90804pt\hbox{\hskip 51.06761pt\hbox{\hbox{\hskip 4.0pt\hbox{$\mathsf{\Gamma\vdash R^{=}(C,c,a,a,refl_{a})=c[a/z]:C[a/x,a/y,refl_{a}/p]}$}\hskip 4.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces

The formation rule
\mathrm{Id}
-Form states that we may form the Id-type
\mathsf{Id_{A}(a,a^{\prime})}
from a type
\mathsf{A}
and terms
\mathsf{a,a^{\prime}:A}
.
The introduction rule
\mathrm{Id}
-Intro defines that there is just one term
\mathsf{refl_{a}}
of the Id-type
\mathsf{Id_{A}(a,a)}
.
Again, the elimination and the computation rules
\mathrm{Id}
-Elim and
\mathrm{Id}
-Comp make sense by the introduction rule.

Definition A.7

(Interpretation of Id-types in CwFs
[
20
]
)
.

The interpretation
\llbracket\_\rrbracket
of Id-types in a CwF
\mathcal{C}
that supports Id is given by:

•

(
=
-Form
)
\llbracket\mathsf{\Gamma\vdash a=_{A}a^{\prime}\ type}\rrbracket\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\mathrm{Id}_{\llbracket\mathsf{A}\rrbracket}\{\langle\overline{\llbracket\mathsf{\Gamma\vdash a:A}\rrbracket},\llbracket\mathsf{\Gamma\vdash a^{\prime}:A}\rrbracket\rangle_{\llbracket\mathsf{A}\rrbracket}\}
;

•

(
=
-Intro
)
\llbracket\mathsf{\Gamma\vdash refl_{A}:a=_{A}a}\rrbracket\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\mathrm{v}_{\mathrm{Id}_{\llbracket\mathsf{A}\rrbracket}}\{\mathrm{Refl}_{\llbracket\mathsf{A}\rrbracket}\circ\overline{\llbracket\mathsf{\Gamma\vdash a:A}\rrbracket}\}
;

•

(
=
-Elim
)
\llbracket\mathsf{\Gamma\vdash R^{=}(C,c,a,a^{\prime},q):C[a/x,a^{\prime}/y,q/p]}\rrbracket\mathrel{\mathchoice{\vbox{\hbox{$\displaystyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle:$}}}{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle:$}}}{=}}\mathcal{R}_{\llbracket\mathsf{A},\mathsf{C}\rrbracket}^{\mathrm{Id}}(\llbracket\mathsf{c}\rrbracket)\{\langle\langle\overline{\llbracket\mathsf{a}\rrbracket},\llbracket\mathsf{a^{\prime}}\rrbracket\rangle_{\llbracket\mathsf{A}\rrbracket},\llbracket\mathsf{q}\rrbracket\rangle_{\llbracket\mathsf{a=_{A}a^{\prime}}\rrbracket}\}
.
